صلبان مطليه بالدهب واخدونا الي كنيسه معظمه عليه وقاها من حديد اخض عجيب دهي واسعه جد مصوره جميعها وهى من خشب وقناديليا الفضه كثير وشموعبها اخضر عجيب وفوق
They led us to a large and high wooden church with iron domes of a beautiful green. It is very spacious and covered with paintings. Its numerous lamps are of silver and its candles are of a wonderful beautiful green. Above the narthex are suspended beautiful bells. One finds there


[p. 637] {#po.26.p637}


[p. 638] {#po.26.p638}
النريكس معلق نواقيسها العجيبه وهاهنا درابزين عالي مشرف الي الخورص حيث يقفوا المصليين وترنموا فى كتنهم البصلتيكا بالارغن وحسبم يدوي كالرعد لان هذة المدينه هي اول مدينه كبير من بلدان القزق وديوتها عاليه حسنه واكثرهم كانوا بيوت اللاد واللهود والادمن بالشبابيك الكثيره بالقماري البلور الملونه ومعلق فوتهم الايقونات واهلبا مكلفين باللباس الحسن وبا تسعة كنايس معظمات بالقباب العاليات على اسم القيامه وخميس الصعود والثالوث المقدس ومولد السيده ونياحها ومار ميخايبل ومار نقولا وعيد الصليب والفصح ايض لان هذه المدينة كانت مركز لللاد وتخت ليم وها من قصورهم الملوكيه كثير
also a high balustrade which dominates the choir where the chanters stand; they perform their chants according to their psalter books, with organ accompaniment; and their voices resound like thunder. It is the first large city in the land of the Cossacks. Their houses are high and pretty. Most belonged to Poles, Jews, and Armenians. They have numerous windows with panes of different colors, with icons suspended above. The inhabitants of the city are richly dressed. The city counts nine large churches, with high domes; these churches are dedicated to the Resurrection, to the Ascension, to the Holy Trinity, to the Nativity of the Virgin, to the Assumption, to Saint Michael, to Saint Nicholas, to the feast of the Exaltation of the holy Cross, to that of Easter, for this city was an important center of the Poles. One also finds there numerous imperial
ونوم السبت حضرنا عندهم القداس وما خرجنا منه آلا وارجلنا عاطله من الوقوف لاننا كما دكرنا ان جميع كنايسيم بلا كراسي وديطولوا كثير في صلواتهم وترائيمهم وقداساتم والخاصه لما بيكرز الكاهن والشماس وبيقولوا المصليين. الواقفين فوق بلسانهم هوسبودي بوميلوي اي كيرباليصن وكل واحده بيترنموها على البصانيكا مقدار ربع ساعه وحبنا علي ابومن بانذس اي نقول كافتنا انهم بيقولها الكاهن فى بلاد القزق وبلاد المصكوف خمسه عشر استبخن وعللي كل استبخن كيراليصن مرا كثيرة بترئيم فضبطنا. انهم بيترنموا كبرياليصن .يا نحو ماية مر وكذلك علي غيرها من الكرز ولاب عن قول رسايلين وانجيلين ! والقاري بيقول الرسايل بلحن احسن من قراآننا نحن الانجيل كلمه كلمه ولابد عن قول بصلمس طو دابيد والبركيدن باستيخوناته كل يوم علبي مدار السنه بترئيم وكذلك بعد قول الانجيل دكصا سي كيريه دكصا صي؟ اول واض ترئيم بالارغن واما حين قوليم أكسيون استين فجميع الكبنه الحاضرين مع اولاد المخورص بيجتمعوا في الوسط وترئيم بترنموها جميعهم من فم واحد من كل قلوهم وعند ذكر بوروديتصا اي والدة الاله جميعهم بيسجدوا للارض ومع هذا فجميمهم مكشوذن الرؤوس3 حتي والكبنه وكنت تنظرهم واقفون في الكنايس كالحجارة لايتحركون واما نحن فكنا نقاسى جهد عظيم حتي ان كانت ارواحنا تمزق من التعب والحصر ولكن كما ذكرنا رآبنا منهم ديانه كليه نلغايه وورع وخشوع انوا يتقاطروا جميعهم وتادروا الترك وتقبيل الصليب من يد سيدنا البطرك ولما كنا ماشين فى الطرقات وكانوا ينظروا الصليب المرفوع على الرمح وهم
palaces. On Saturday we attended mass; when we left there, our feet were weary from having remained standing; for as we have already said, their churches are devoid of stalls and their prayers as well as their chants and their masses last a long time, especially when the priests and the deacon say the 'synaptie' and the chanters respond in their language 'gospodi pomilui,' that is to say: 'Lord, have mercy on us,' and each response was sung according to oriental music and that for a quarter of an hour. We noticed that 'eipomen pantes,' 'let us all say,' was said by the priests of the land of the Cossacks and the Muscovites, that is to say in fifteen stichoi and that at each stichos one sang 'have mercy on us' several times, and we counted that they had sung 'Lord, have mercy on us' about a hundred times and likewise for the rest of the 'synaptie.' They always say the 'pericopes' of the epistles and the gospels and the reader reads the epistle in a melody more beautiful than that which we use for the gospel, not failing to detach each word. They also read the psalms of David and sing the 'prokimenon' with its stichoi every day of the year. Before and after the reading of the Gospel, they sing 'Glory to You, Lord, Glory to You' while accompanying themselves on the organ. When they sing the 'Axion,' all the priests present as well as the choirboys group themselves in the middle of the church and sing in unison and with soul. And when one mentions the 'Bogoroditsa,' that is to say the Mother of God, everyone prostrates themselves to the ground, bareheaded, even the priests: one can see them motionless in the churches like stones. As for us, we found ourselves subjected to a painful ordeal and we were overwhelmed with fatigue and malaise. As we have said, we observed a powerful religious sentiment, a great piety and humility among these inhabitants. Everyone hastened and hurried to receive the blessing and to kiss the cross from the hand of my lord the patriarch. When we passed on the roads and they saw the cross raised on a lance while they were busy


[p. 639] {#po.26.p639}


[p. 640] {#po.26.p640}
مشغولين بحصادهم كانوا يلتفتوا نحو المشرق مع النسآة والاولاد وصلبوا علي وجوهيم واما الرجال والشباب فكانوا يرموا مناجلهم واشغالهم وبركضوا عدوا للتبارك من سيدنا البطرك وبخاصه المسافرون كانوا من بعيد ينزلوا من علي نخيلبم ومن قاروصاتهم وتحولوا من الطريق وصطفوا وهم مكشوفين الروس الي ان يجوز سيدنا البطرك بقوحيته علييم! وسجدوا للادض ولاتوا يقبلوا الصليب وبسينه ونصرفوا نعاود ثم -خرجنا من القداس قرب .الظهر والبروكونيكوس سفعان المذكور وكاخيته رافضشين كتاف سيدنا البطرك حسب عادتهم الي ان دخل به لبيته وعمانا له آجيازس وصلي عليه وعلى .حريته افاشين استغفار وجلسنا للطرانازا ثم قمنا وهو يودعنا لمنزؤلنا
at the harvest, they turned toward the East with their wives and their children and made the sign of the cross. The men and the young people threw down their sickles, left their work, and ran to be blessed by my lord the patriarch; in particular, we saw from afar travelers descend from their horses or their carriages and, moving away from the middle of the road, line up bareheaded on the side, until my lord the patriarch passed in his carriage: then they prostrated themselves to the ground, went to kiss the cross and the hand of my lord, and left again. Let us return to our subject. We left mass when it was near noon. The 'polcovnic' Simeon and his lieutenant, conforming to the habit, supported my lord the patriarch by the shoulders until they had made him enter his house. We performed the service of holy water for him and the patriarch read to him as well as to his wife the prayers of absolution and we sat down to eat. After
ورحلنا من هومانو وودعنا لخارج البلد وعين معنا ايض عكر صحتنا ومشينا ميل واحد واتينا الي بازار آخن بحصار وقلعه اسمه كراسنوبولا وخرجوا لاقوا لنا حسب عادتهم لانه كان دايماً من حين نقوم نحن من البلد يسبقنا واحد من العسكرية الذين منابمكتوب من البروكونيكوس الي جميع حكمه لمهم بقدوبنا حتي يهيروا الاقامه ماكول ومشروب يكفى لجميع من مننا وكنا نحو ارسون نفر نحن وخدامنا ورفقتنا رئيسا الدياره وخدامهم وكذلك روس خيل يرسلوا العرابانات ايض ويحصدوا ليم حشيش اخض لان كما ذكرنا ان صيف هذ البلاد جميعه ربيع وازهار الي ايام تشرين وكنا نتعجب من زهر الربيع كثير وكانوا يلاقونا بالخيز لخارج الللد حب عادتهم لاحل البركه وكذلك كلما كنا جلسنا على المايده يوضعوا الخبز اولا قبل كل شني ثم ان اهل البلد المدكور خرجوا لاقونا كالعاده كما ذكرنا الي خارج اللد بمشوار وكان حين تقبل! السناحجق والصلبان ينزل سيدنا البطرك حسب عادته دايم توزعا ليم ويمشى بالمنثيه مشوار كبير الي بينما يدخلونا للكنيسه ثم نخرج منها كذلك للمنزول والصليب الذي علي الربح مركوز على باب الدار
we rose and he accompanied us to our dwelling. We left Uman, he accompanied us to bid us farewell until the exit of the city after having given us an escort. We traveled a mile and arrived at another town defended by a fortress and a citadel, called Krasnopolka. The inhabitants came according to custom to meet us. When we left a city, one of the soldiers of our escort left in advance carrying a letter from the 'polcovnic' addressed to all the officials of his jurisdiction, announcing our arrival, so that they could prepare lodging, food, and drink for us and our suite; it comprised about forty people, that is to say our persons, our servants, and our companions, two hegumens and their servants. In addition, the officials had to prepare horses, to send carriages, to have green fodder cut, for, as we have already said, all summer in this country is a spring and its flowers last until the month of October. We were very amazed by these spring flowers. The inhabitants received us at the gate of the city with bread according to their habit to obtain the blessing. Likewise, each time we had lunch, the first thing that was served to us was bread. The inhabitants of this city came out to meet us, at a certain distance outside the city. When the banners and the crosses approached, my lord the patriarch descended, according to his habit, out of respect for them and he walked for a long time dressed in his 'mandyas' until they had led him to the church, from where we left to go to our dwelling. During all this time, the cross hoisted on a lance was planted in the ground
ثم انهم بعد ان دخلونا لكنيستهم التي على اسم مار ميخاييل رحلنا بالحال ومشينا ميل اضر واتبنا عشيه الاحد الخامن بعد النصرة الى بلد كبين جد ثلاث حصارات
before the door of the house. Then they made us enter the church dedicated to Saint Michael. We hastened to leave again and after having traveled another mile, we arrived, on the evening of the fifth Sunday after Pentecost, in a very large


[p. 641] {#po.26.p641}


[p. 642] {#po.26.p642}
وثلاث. اقلاع. اسمه ماقيكًا بارينة بحرات المياد كاد وبيو» سقلمات. حبنات اكات لبود والارمن وبه اربعة كنايس على اسم التجلي الألبي والسيده ومار ميخائيل ومار نقولا معظمات عليه ثم وخارجه دير على اسم مار حجرجس عمالين بيعمروه جديداً
city provided with three fortresses and three citadels, called Ma(n)kovka, with four large ponds. There are large and beautiful houses belonging to Jews and Armenians. One also finds there four churches; they are dedicated to the divine Transfiguration, to Our Lady, to Saint Michael, and to Saint Nicholas. Outside the city there is a convent under reconstruction, it is dedicated to Saint George.
وخرجوا لاقوا لنا حسب العاده الكبنه والشمامسه لخارج اليلد بالسناجق والصلبان والشموع الكثيرة ودخلونا للكنيسه وهم يترنموا الي ان أكرز البروطوباباس وذكر اسم سيدنا البطرك الانطاكي واسم مطرانبم سلفسترس والختمان زنوبيون والملك الكسيون حسب العاده دام وخرجوا قدامنا ببدلاتهم وبالشموع وهم يترنموا الى ان_تزونا فى المتزول وبارك عليهم وانصرفوا اعلم ان في هذه بلاد القزرق خمر ما فيه ولكنهم بيشربوا ما الشعير المطبوخ اللديد وهو طيب حد وكنا نشريه بدل الخمر كيف تعمل الا ان ما الشعير ليد بارد على المعدة بخاصه في ايام الصيف وم العسل المطبوخ ايض وهو بيسكر اما العرق الذي من الفريزا وهى شبه زنوانة الجنطه بيطبخوه هو كثير ورخيص جد ثم اصبحنا نهار الاحد المدكور حضرنا عندهم الاورترن ثم القداس ثم مضيت تفرجت علي سرايات حاكم هذه المدينه الذي كان اسمه كالينوسكه هذا كان من كبار حكام اللاد لان معظميم كانوا اربعه فالواحد كان اسمه بوطوسكه والتانى كان اسمه كومصارى وهو الذي ذكرنا صفة قتله فى اول حكم القرق كان عنده مايتى الف وهذا الثالث كالينوسكه وكانت هومانو الى حد راشكو مع هذه اليلد وغيرها شتّى كثير له وماكه وكان عند من خاص خيل الترك الثمينه اربعون راس خيل وكان عنده من خاص غلمانه الابطال اثني عشر الف كلهم باللباس الثمين واما غيرهم من الساكر الالوف وكان كثير فلما جا اخميل حاربه فانكس واخدوه التطر يسيراً الى بلادهم مع بوطوسكه فارسل فاسيلي بيك الغضان وضمنهم حتى استفكوا نفونهم بثمانين الف دهب كل واحد والرايع ارخن كان اسمه فيشنوفاسكه كان حاكم ثالث قاطع نهر نيبروس وكان مقصد فاسيلي باستنقادهم انه يعمل معهم حميل ليكافوه فيما بعد باحسن معه ثم كافوه للان اللا ارسلوا معونه لعدوه ستافانى فوشوضا وهو على حرب قلعه صتجاوًا واصابوا تيموتاون
The priests and the deacons came to meet us, as usual outside the city with banners, crosses, and numerous candles. They introduced us into the church, singing, until the archpriest had said the 'synaptie' and pronounced the name of my lord the patriarch of Antioch, the name of their metropolitan Sylvester, those of the hetman Zenobe and of the emperor Alexis, always according to custom. They left preceding us, dressed in their priestly vestments, carrying candles and singing until they had led us to our dwelling. The patriarch blessed them and they withdrew. Note. In this land of the Cossacks, there is no wine, the inhabitants drink there a pleasant and very good beer. We drank it in place of wine. How to do otherwise? for good sweet beer refreshes the stomach, especially during the summer. There is also mead which intoxicates; one also makes brandy manufactured with 'fariza' (plant) which resembles wheat tares. This drink obtained by cooking is in abundance and very cheap. That Sunday, we rose early in the morning and attended matins, then mass. Afterward, we went to visit the palace of the governor of this city, a governor who was called Kalinovski; he was one of the four greatest Polish governors: the first was called Potovski, the second was named 'commissioner,' it is he whose manner of death we described at the beginning of the reign of the Cossacks and who had under his orders two hundred thousand men, the third was this Kalinovski. His government included Uman as far as the border of Râscov, with this last city and many others that belonged to him personally, he had forty Turkish horses of great price, a guard of twelve thousand elite men, all dressed in rich uniforms, he had in addition a troop of several thousand soldiers. When Hmilnitzki waged war on him, Kalinovski was defeated and the Tatars took him prisoner with Potovski to their country. Then Basil, prince of Moldavia, sent to ransom them by paying for each twenty thousand ducats. The fourth governor was called Vichnevski, one of the three governors of the banks of the Dnieper. In saving their lives, Basil had had the intention of doing them a service in order to be rewarded by them one day, and the Poles rewarded him indeed, for they sent help to his enemy Stephen Voivode who was besieging the citadel of Luceava and they killed Timothy, son of Hmilnitzki, his son-in-law, with a cannon shot as we have recounted: his hope was disappointed. Then Hmilnitzki became very angry against


[p. 643] {#po.26.p643}


[p. 644] {#po.26.p644}
ابن اخميل صهرنا بالمدفع! وقتلوه كما ذكرنا خاب ظنه فاغتاض عايه اخميل سبب ذلك كثيراً للان هولاء الاثنين حجاآا الى بلادهم وجددوا عاي اخميل والقزق حررًا ايض الا أن تيموتاورس الشجاع حرج عليهم وحاريم وقتليم. سه "ومسا دكرغم من الأرشن
Stephen because of this proceeding, for these two had gone into their country and waged war again on Hmilnitzki and the Cossacks, but Timothy, renowned for his bravery, came out to meet them, fought them, killed them with his sword, and erased their name from the earth.
واما سراياته فبي قاطع المدينه تظبر من بعد كثير لعلوها وبينها وبين اللد نهر كبير وبحر عظيم جد عليباة جسس وهى قلعه في راس رقعه عاليه وداين ما يدورها خندق عظيم وسور من خشب براني متقود لاجل هجم الخيل وسور اخر داخل مضبوط وقدام الباب مدافع كبار وعلي الاصداغ من هنا وهنا مدافع صغار وداين ما يدور القلعه ابرلج خشب مثينه ووسط الدار فحه كبيره وفي صدرها بيت سرآي معظم جميعه من خشب الملتول الصلب الذي لا يحترق المجلى اربعه اوجاهة المصقول المضبوط الذي لا يظهر له لزاق لكذلك تحسب البيت والحايط قطعه واحدة الطول العريض وطول كل لوح اي خكيه مربعه خمسون كيرا واكثر كما قسناه وعرضها اربعة اشبار وعلي قدر عظم السراي داير ما يدورها اربعة الواح من الطول للطول الى السقف وببذا المكان من المخادع كثير وفوتها بيوت آخري وطبقات علي بعضها بعض ما القباطير والافران اي اماكن النيران فى الشتا فى معظمه جد وعلوها اعلا من شجن السرو وبكثير نافده الى اعالى الطبقات واسعه جد وبقرب هذه السراي اسطبلات الخيل المعظعه ب"
The palaces of Kalinovski are found on the other side of the city, and because of their height one can see them from a great distance. Between these palaces and the city there is a large river and a very large pond on which there is a bridge. It is a citadel on the summit of a hill, surrounded on all sides by a large ditch and an outer wooden rampart, impenetrable to cavalry attacks. Inside there is another rampart without intervals. Before the gate of the city, there are large cannons and to the right and left of the doorposts small cannons. In the perimeter of the citadel there are solid wooden bastions. In the middle of the courtyard there is a large square. At the back of this courtyard is the large palace built of solid oak wood, which cannot catch fire, polished on its four faces, well leveled and exactly assembled, so that one cannot see the joints; it seems that the house and the walls are of a single piece in length and breadth. The length of each squared timber or beam is fifty spans and more, according to the measurement we took of it, and the width is four spans. The whole perimeter of this vast palace is formed of four planks superimposed in length, up to the roof. In this place, there are numerous rooms, above there are other rooms and floors superimposed on each other. The chimneys and the ovens where one lights the fire during the winter are very large. Their height is greater than that of a cypress. They reach up to the upper floors and
صعدنا الي اعالي السراي الاخري الصفيه دهي فوق باب القلعه بسلالم تر وهي سرايات فوق بعضها بعض وفي دايرها الشابيك الكثيره! وهي على صفة؟ عمارة جيل مار سمعان عندنا وبلاد المعرة بغاية الزرافه والنقوثى وربما يظبر للجالس في الاعلتي من مسيرة يوم وكثر ما يقصدد من الامكن والطرقات وهي الآن خراب لا يها احدً تبكي علي اصحايا ثم نزلنا ومضينا الي كنيسه العظمي وهي مثابل القلعه على اسم مار نقولاوس لان هكذا سموها القزق الآن وعملين بيعمروا في قابيا وهي من معظم الكنايس في التعنيد والرفعه والعلو والكبر وداخلها دخلات شكل صليب اي الخورصين فني داخباة ثم اليكل المعظم الكبير وداخله حيث الكائدر اربعه عواميد خشب كار للغايه محفورات منقوشات
are very spacious. Near this palace are the large stables. We then climbed to the very top of the other palace which is the summer palace; it is located above and a little behind the gate of the citadel, it has many steps. These are superimposed palaces and on their faces open many windows, in the style of the construction of Djabal Mar Simaan of our country and of the city of Maarra. It is very elegant and sculpted and allows the traveler placed at the summit to see at a distance of a day and more the localities and the roads where he will have to go. Now, these palaces are in ruins and they shelter no one, they seem to weep for their former masters. Then we descended and went into a large church, located opposite the citadel and dedicated to Saint Nicholas, for that is how the Cossacks named it, they are rebuilding its domes. It is one of the main churches, as much in terms of solidity as in terms of height and its size. Inside there are (transepts) which give the shape of a cross to the building; these are the two choirs; then comes the large sanctuary. One sees there the episcopal throne with four very large columns in


[p. 645] {#po.26.p645}


[p. 646] {#po.26.p646}
مدهيات جميعهم. لا يفرقوا عن الصياغه وفوقهم صفة .قب واما الثمبلن عمالين بيشرعوا فيه جديداً وفي الخورصين اماكن لوقوف" الكبنه والمصليين من علي زمن اللاد ابقوهم وجميع عمدهم وخشهم وحيث يوضعوا الكتب منقوش مدهب وفوق النرتكسن الكبير اماكن بدرابزين مشرفه على الخورص حيث يقفوا المصليين يترنموا بالارغن .واما علو درج هذا المكان فكثير جد وخارج باب الكنيسه برج النواقين وداين مايدور الدار سور خشب وجناين
wood; they are openwork, sculpted, and gilded like pieces of goldsmithery, they are surmounted by a dome. The symbol was rebuilt recently. In the two choirs, there are stalls for the priests and the chanters; they are from the time of the Poles. They have been preserved, as well as their supports and their wood. The place where one deposits the books is sculpted and gilded. Above the large narthex there are galleries with balustrades which overlook the choir; there stand the chanters who perform their pieces with organ accompaniment. The staircase which leads to the galleries is very high. Outside the door of the church there is a bell tower and all around the courtyard there is a wooden fence and gardens. Likewise, all the houses of this city are large and made of planed and polished wood, for as we have said, these houses were those of the Armenians and the Jews that the Cossacks had exterminated. They had taken their money and their goods, as well as their houses, their properties, their gardens, and their lands. And this they had deserved, for during the reign
وكذلك جميع بيوت هذه المدينه معظمة بالخشب المجلي المصقول لانهم كما ذكرنا كانوا بيوت الادمن واليهود الذين قطعوا القرق الارض من تحتهم وحازوا امواليم وغنايمهم مع بيوتهم واملاكهم. وبساتينهم واراضيهم وهم مستحقين لذلك لانم كانوا في زمن دولة. اللاد هم الحكام والولاد والكمركجيه وكانوا مستعيدينهم للغايه فلما ملكهم الباري ابادوا دكرهم من الارض فيا لها من امه ماركه ومن بلاد مباركه هذا عظم مقدارها ما بها أممى من غير مكة اصلًا ألا انها صافى ارتودكسيين مؤينين دنين يا لها من غيرة روح قدسيّه ومن مله طاهره على التحقيق ارتودكسيه .يا لطوبا لاعيننا وما نظرت ولاداننا وما سمعت لقلونا وما انسرت وابتهجت لانبم بعد ان كانوا في اليسر والعبوديه هم الان عايشين بالفرح والسرور
The Poles, the Jews, and the Armenians were the judges, the authorities, and the customs officers, and they had enslaved the Cossacks. When the Creator placed the Cossacks in possession of their land, they erased their memory from the earth. Oh, what a blessed nation and country! What constitutes its principal value is that there is not a single pagan to be found there and that all its inhabitants are pure Orthodox, believers, and religious. What zeal in this people for the purity and holiness of the spirit and the Orthodox truths! Blessed be our eyes for having seen, our ears for having heard, and our hearts for having rejoiced, for after having been in captivity and slavery, they now live in joy and freedom!
والحرية وابتنوا الكنايى الكثوليكيه وانشوا الايقونات الجليله والقونسطاسات. الطاهزة الالبيه والسناجق والاعلام الصليبيه لاننا ننظر الي كل كنيسه. احسن واعظم واجمل واعلا واكبر من غيرها وكل قونسطاس وثمبلن وايقونات ابهج واشرف من غيرها حتي وكنايس القري احسن واحسن ودخلوا لمجاهرة الدين بعشق جديد والي التعليم والقرااه ولبصلتيكا بشوق شديد ترنيم اللديّد وهم يستاهلوا لانهم مستسيرين بغاية القناعه اكل مهما اتفق ولبس مهما اتفق
They have built cathedral churches, displayed magnificent icons, established holy iconostases, and raised banners and flags adorned with crosses. Each church we see appears to us more beautiful, larger, more magnificent, and taller than the last. All the iconostases, all the symbols, and all the icons are more magnificent and more wonderful than one another; the village churches themselves are more beautiful than one another. This people manifested a new ardor in the practice of their religion, even learning to read and sing in a very pleasant manner. They deserve their happiness, for they are sober and modest, content with what is found both for food and
ثم اصبحنا نهار الاثنين وهو ١١ حزيران ومشيئا ميلين واتينا الي بازار اخر كبير وهو بين حيلين بحصارات والقلعه علي حافة الجبل والبحرة كبيرة .جاربه في الوادي وعلي سكرها أرعة طواحين بحركات عجييه وكذلك في سايرطواحين هذه البلاد لان مجري الميلا بيتحدر وبيدير النواعير الخارحجه وقلوبها بتدين الطواحين لطحن الحنطه م
for clothing. On Monday morning, June 19, we traveled two miles and arrived at another large fortified town situated between two mountains. The citadel is at the edge of the mountain; there is a large pond, the water of which flows into the valley. On the edge of the dike, there are four mills with wonderful mechanisms like all the mills in the country: the watercourse descends and sets the outer wheels in motion, and their axles set the mills in motion for the grinding of wheat. To crush the 'fariza' and barley, there are pestles, which, moved by a


[p. 647] {#po.26.p647}


[p. 648] {#po.26.p648}
احدقات الفريزا والشعير حركات مدقات طالعه نازله فى الاجران فالفريزا بياخدوها مدقوقه مكسوره لطبخ العرق والشعير لطبخه واستخراج ما ثم مدقات اخري للكتان الذي بيزرعوة وبيشتغلوه قمصان وبين انواعير من خارج اجران كبار من الخشب كانوا يدلكوا بهم الجوخ في زمن اللاه بعد جري المآ عليه ايام كثيرة
mechanism, rise and fall into the troughs. One takes the crushed and broken 'fariza' for the manufacture of brandy and the barley for the manufacture of beer. There are other pestles for the flax that is cultivated and from which shirts are made. Between the outer wheels, there are large wooden troughs where, in the time of the Poles, cloth was fulled after the water current had passed over it for several days.
اود واسم البلد المدكور بوكتي وبه ثلاث كناين على اسم نياج السّده ومار ميخاييل ومار ايليا وبعد ان لاقوا لنا كالعاده بالسناحجق والكبنه ودحلونا للكنيسه وخرحنا طلبوا من سيدنا البطرك ان .يكرين ليم كئيسه رابعه حديده كانوا بعد ما كملوها واخدونا اليها بباعوت ودخلنا اليها وعمل سيدنا البطرك اجيازمس وكرينها جميعها وللبيكل ووطد المايد والمديح وكرسم بالميرون الالبي وصلي عليهم الافاشين المعلومه وسماها باسم الميلاد الشريف ثم كرس جميع الحاضرين بالماء
But let us return to our subject. The name of the city mentioned above is Buki. There are three churches there dedicated to the Assumption of the Virgin, to Saint Michael, and to Saint Elijah. After coming to meet us as usual with banners and priests, they had us enter a church, and after we had come out, the inhabitants asked my lord the patriarch to consecrate for them a fourth church that had been recently built and which they had not yet finished; we were led there in a solemn procession; we entered it; my lord the patriarch performed the service of holy water and sprinkled the whole church with it; he consecrated the sanctuary, the altar, and the prothesis with the holy chrism, recited the prayers appropriate for the consecration, and dedicated the church to the Nativity of Christ; then he sprinkled all those present
لاننا كنا كلما دخلنا لقريه ولمدينه كانوا بلاقونا الكبنه فى وعآ اجيازمس فكان لما ينزل من القوجيه ويقبل الصلبان والابقونات التي في يديهم ثم يقبلوا هم صليبه ويمينه ايض كانوا يقدموا له وعآ الاجيازمس فكان يطرش على الحاضرين بالريحان شكل صليب ثم اذا دخلنا للكنيسه وبعد الكرازه وختم الصلاه كانوا يوضعوا له كرسي لبكرس جميع الحاضرين واحد بعد واحد بالاجيازمس ثم وهو خارج يطرش على جميع اهل البلده عن .يميه وشماله الى ان نصل للمنزول ومن حين دخنا الي بلاد القزق ثم الي حين خروجنا منها كنا ست قاروصات وخيل معونه لحمل حوايجنا من بلد الي بلد حسب عادتهم لان خيانا كانت وقفت في هذا الطريق الطويل
with the holy water. Each time we entered a village or a city, the priests came to meet us with a vessel of holy water, and the patriarch, upon getting out of the carriage, would kiss the crosses and icons they held in their hands; the priests would kiss his cross and his right hand, then they would present him with the vessel of holy water, and he would sprinkle the faithful with it, making the sign of the cross with a sprig of basil. In every church we went to, after the 'synapte' and the end of the divine service, a tetrapodion was set up so that he could sprinkle the faithful one after the other with holy water. After which, he would sprinkle the inhabitants of the city to his right and to his left until we arrived at our dwelling. From our entry into the land of the Cossacks until our departure, we requisitioned carriages and horses to transport our luggage from one city to another, according to the custom of the country, for our horses were no longer walking because of the length of the road.
ثم خرحنا منها بالحال ومشينا ارسة اميال وكان طريقنا جميعه في هذا اليوم حرش عظيم شجر الملول " وعمالين بيقطعوا به وبيحرقوا اصوله وبيحرتوه وبيزرعوه زراعات وهكذا فعلهم فى جميع هذه البلاد لان في زمن اللاه كما اخرونا ما كان المسافر ينظر الشمس من عظم الحرش وكثرته لكثرة ما كانوا اللاه معتنين به ومربينيه شبه البساتين كما ذكرنا لاحتياجم اليه في عمارة الاسوار والحصون والبيوت فلما تملكوا القرق اتقاسموا الاداضي وعملوا السياجات والحدود وهم بيقطعوا ليلا وناراً
Then we left immediately and traveled four miles through large oak forests where they were clearing and burning the roots to put the land under cultivation; this is how it is currently done in this whole country, whereas in the time of the Poles, as we were told, the traveler did not see the sun because of the vast extent of the forests. Indeed, as we have already said, the Poles took great care of the forests and maintained them as one does for gardens; they were necessary for them for the construction of ramparts, fortifications, and houses. When the Cossacks conquered the country and divided it among themselves, they made hedges, drew boundaries, and cut down trees day and night.


[p. 649] {#po.26.p649}


[p. 650] {#po.26.p650}
ثم اننا حينا عشيه. الي بلد كبير بحصار ايض وامياا وبساتين لان هذ اللاد المباركه صفة الريائه لعظم حسنها وعمارها اسمه ليسيتكا وبه اربعة كنايس علي اسم التجلي الالبي وهي التي دخلونا الها وعلي باها ناقون نحاسن جديد كيين جد سما حنه من! سفر ساعه وقبابها من نكا حديد والكنيسه الكبري عندهم بيسموها الكاتوليكيه ثم الثلاث كنايس الأخري علي اسم السّده ومار ميخابيل ومار تقولا ثم ودير كبير علي اسم الثالوث المقدس ونظرنا فى هذه الكنيسه ابقونة السيده وهى عروس. مكللّه لاننا نظرنا ايقوناتها جايه حجايه مصوّر أعدري بنت بتول يخدود حمر ومعلقين فى هذا الكنيسه ايض تريا من قرن بقر ويحش علي مثال الذي ظهر فيه المسيح لاسطانيوس وخاطه ومن هذا البلدة .ارسانا الى المحفوظ من الله اخبيل الختمان دينوعيوى. مكتوب: عليه بَِدُومًا حب العاد لان كان مقيم باكر بعيد عن هذلا البلدب بارزيعه أميال كبار ثم قمنا نهار الثلثا ومشينا ميل كبير واتينا الي بازار آخر بحصار وخندق جديد وبحرة ما ايض اسمه مادفادكا وبه ثلاث كنايسن على السيده ومار تقولا ومار جرجس وبيصير به ناير أي بغ وشري علي عيد مولد .يوحنا المعمدان في رابع وعشرون هذا شبر «حزيران ثم قمنا منها ومشينا ميلين اخري كبار في حرش عظيم بين لين والطريق شق عسر في وادي ومقفله الطرقات كل قليل بخشب متقود لمنع هجم الخيل ثم من علي اليمين والشمال بيوت عامرلا مقدار ثاثمايه وعندهم في سفل الوادي رابح داح نحو عشرلا بحرات للسمك فمن الواحده جاربه للاخري اي من فايض سكرها فايض الاخري الي الاخري وعليهم الطواحين وعلى سكوتتهم مزروع شجر الصفصاف كثير اعلم ان علي بحرات جميع هذه البلاد زهن النوفن اصفر العادةة ثم ابيض مكس شني كثير جد وما كنا نتعجب آلا من كثربه قناياهم وطيورهم اي الدجاج والوز والط ودحاج البندي كثير الذي دايرين في البرادي والاحراش يرعوا بعيد عن المدن والقري كثير وبين الحروش والدروات بيبيضوا حيث لا من يبحث ولا من يطلب لكثرتم لان ما فى هذ اللاد ولا بيعرفوا لا نمس ولا وحش كاسر ولا هوام الارض قطعا حتى واذا صدف في الاحيان حيه كما نظرنا في طرقنا من بلاد الفلاخ الي تخت المصكوف حبه واحده لاغير وقتلاها آلا لا فلا لم ولا من يسرق ولا من بينبب
In the evening we arrived at another large fortified city, having water and gardens, for this blessed city is like a pomegranate in its beauty and prosperity; it is called Lisianka. It possesses four churches: the one dedicated to the Divine Transfiguration is the one into which they had us enter. At the gate, there is a very large bronze bell whose sound we heard from an hour's distance. The domes are covered with tin. The largest church among them is called a cathedral. There are also three other churches dedicated to the Holy Virgin, to Saint Michael, and to Saint Nicholas; there is also a large convent dedicated to the Holy Trinity. In this church, we saw the icon of the Holy Virgin representing her as a crowned bride; we have seen her icons everywhere, representing her as a Virgin with rosy cheeks. In this church, they have also hung a chandelier made of the horns of a wild ox similar to those of the stag that appeared to Saint Eustace and where Christ spoke to that saint. From this city, we sent a letter to the Hetman Hmilnitzki Zenobius, may God preserve him!, to announce our arrival, for he was camping with his army four large miles from this city. We left on Tuesday, traveled a large mile, and arrived in another fortified town with a recently dug ditch and a pond; this town is called Madfadka. This locality has three churches dedicated to Saint Nicholas, to Saint George, and to the Virgin. On the occasion of the feast of the Nativity of Saint John the Baptist, which falls on June 24, a 'panayir' or fair is held there. Then we left and traveled two other large miles, through large forests situated between two mountains and by a difficult and narrow path in a valley: the passage is well blocked from distance to distance by palisades built to prevent a cavalry attack. To the right and left, there were well-built houses to the number of three hundred. In the bottom of the valley, there is a series of about ten fish ponds communicating with one another, with the overflow of the dams spilling into one another. On their banks, one sees mills and numerous willows. Remark. On the surface of the ponds in this country, there is a yellow-colored flower usually called a 'water lily,' then it whitens and becomes pommeled; it is found in very great quantity. We were very surprised at the sight of the multitude of their chicken coops and farmyards filled with hens, geese, ducks, and Muscovy ducks that roam the fields and forests to graze, far from the cities and villages, among the trees and under shelters; the birds lay eggs there; no one bothers them, nor disturbs them, so numerous are they; for in this region one knows neither weasel, nor beast of prey, nor reptile, nor even a snake. During our trip from Wallachia to Moscow, we saw only one snake, which we killed; those one might encounter are harmless. In this country, there are neither thieves nor brigands.


[p. 651] {#po.26.p651}


[p. 652] {#po.26.p652}
اعلم اننا كنا نتظر في بيوت هذه البلاد من الناس الي البهايم والطيور فنتعجب جد من الخيرات التي عندهم لانك تنظر في بيت الرحل يا هذا عشرا اولاد شعور رؤسيم ابيض وكنا ندعوهم الشيوخ لتنلم بياضه علي رؤس بعضهم بعض بل وأكثر من العشرة ولكن ماكان يزيد تعجنا من انهم على روس ببضهم بعض لانهم كانو يخرجوا خنادج منائليم يتفرجوا علينا لكن نحن كنا نتفرج عليهم فتنظر الكبير فى الطرف لاطا منه بشبر بقريه والاوطا والاوطا الى الاصغر اخرهم تبارك خلاقهم فماذا نقول عن هذا الامة الماركه قتل في الس منهم هذد السنين كرات والتطر كانوا ياخدوا منهم! الافات والطاعون فما كانوا يعرفوه ولكن حدث عندهم في هذد السنتين اخد منهم ريوات الي جنات النعيم ومع هذا فهم مثل عداد النمل أكثر من النجوم ريما ان الامراة منهم بتحيدل وتولد في السنه ثلاث اربع مرات وكل مره بتولد ثلائه اربعه حمله ولكن الاصح كما قلوا انا ان ماء هذه اللاد ل يترك امراد عاقرا وهذا امر ظاهر صدقا عند كل احد مجر
Remark. We observed in the houses of this country, both among humans and among animals and birds, a surprising well-being; thus, one sees in one and the same family children with almost white hair, so that we called them old men because of the color of their hair. What do I say? One counted more than ten, and what was most surprising was that they followed each other closely by rank of height. When they came out of the house to look at us, we, for our part, looked at them with curiosity; one could have seen the tallest at the end and next to him his younger brother, a span shorter than him, and so on, down to the smallest, the last of them. May the Creator be blessed! What to say about this blessed people, who in the course of these last years have been massacred by the hundreds of thousands, not counting the great number of those whom the Tatars had taken into captivity and whom calamities and the plague had decimated, without recalling what happened to them in the course of these last two years, where a very great number left for heaven! But despite these losses, they are as numerous as ants and more numerous than the stars. Could it be perhaps that their women conceive and give birth three or four times a year and that each woman brings into the world three or four children at a time? But the truth is, as we were told, that in this country there does not remain a sterile woman; it is a fact established and verified by experience.
واما دواهم ومواشيهم فتنظر في بيت الرجل يا هذا تبارك الخلاق عشرة اصناف حيوانات فول الخيل تان البقر ثانا الغنم رابع الممزي كانم غزلان خامنً الخنازير سادسً الدجاج سابع الوز تامنًا الط تاسعا دحاج البندي الكشير وحضيم عندهم الحمام عاشراً له اماكن فوق سقوف بيوتهم ثم الكلاب وما كنا نتعجب الا من صنوف اجناس الخنازير الملونه واشكاليم لانهم سود ويض وحمر وشقر وصقر وزرق ثم اسود منقط بأبيض واذرق منقط بأحس واحمر منقط باصفر وابيض منقط باشقر وحضهم بلق وحضهم خطوط خطوط اشكال ونا ما كنا نفرح نضحك علي اولادهم الصغار وما كنا نقدر نضط واحدا منهم اصلًا لان بلا شك ان في بطونهم شياطين ونزيطوا مثل الزيبق فليم صوت يصوي لعيد واما الانثى فانها تولد فى السنه ثلاث مرات فاذا كان اول ولادتها من عمرها تولد احد عش واحد تمام وتائى ولادة تسعه ثالث مر سيعه واربة سه وخامنة ثلئه ونادس مرا من عمرها واحد اعني بالفرد ثم لا تعود تحبل قطمًً وتصير عاقر للدببح وهم بيدبحوا الدكوره وبيبقوا الانات وليم رعلا وحدهم واما الدجاج والوز والبط كل صنف بصنف وحده واما اصناف زرعاتهم فعجيبه وكثيره اشكال اشكال ياتي دكرها فى محلّه
As for the mounts and livestock of this country, one sees about ten species near the house of each inhabitant, may the Creator be blessed! First, horses; second, oxen; third, sheep; fourth, goats similar to gazelles; fifth, pigs; sixth, hens; seventh, geese; eighth, ducks; ninth, very numerous Muscovy ducks; tenth, among some, domestic pigeons that stay on the roofs of houses, then dogs. We found nothing more astonishing than the variety of pigs and their colors, for one finds black, white, red, russet, yellow, gray, then black spotted with white, gray spotted with red, red spotted with yellow, and white spotted with russet; some are variegated with white, and others are striped with different colors. The sight of the piglets amused us greatly. We could not catch a single one; they undoubtedly had the devil in them; they moved like quicksilver, and their piercing cries were heard from afar. The sow gives birth three times a year: the first litter is eleven piglets; the second, nine; the third, seven; the fourth, five; the fifth, three; the sixth, a single piglet; after which she remains sterile and is only good for the butcher. In general, they kill the pigs and keep the sows. There are shepherds or keepers separately for the herds of pigs. As for the hens, geese, and ducks, each species is kept separately. As for the cereals of this country, they are very beautiful, and there exist very varied species of them; it will be spoken of elsewhere for each of them.


[p. 653] {#po.26.p653}


[p. 654] {#po.26.p654}
نعاود الي وصف الثاثماية بيت في الوادي ان عندهم كنيتان واسم هذا المكان المدكور ايساي وكل بيت من هذد البيوت وحوله جنينه وسياحها شجر الكراز والداماصكينا وغير وارضبا مزروعه ملفوف وجزر وفجل وبقدنس وض وغير ذلك وجميع هذا الحرش المدكور محتاطين عليه بسياجات كل جانب اناس ثم لما تخلمنا من الحرش والطريق الضيق مشينا ميل اخر تمام الاربعه في هذا اليوم. واقبلنا علي بلد كبير بحصار وقلعه اسمه بونموصلافي وقطعنا النهر العريض بقريه العسماة روش بالمراكب وكانو حينعد كبنه البلد المدكور السئة ببدلاتهم بالسناحق واولاد الخويص مع بقيه الشعب ومع العسكر بيرق الخثمان المحب للمسيح زينوبيوس الحربي وهو من ضرآي! اسوذ واصضر خطوط خطوط بصليبه جميعهم مستنظرينا علي حافة النهر ولما صعد سيدنا اللطرك للارض سجدوا جميعهم قدامه وقبل صلائهم وايقوناتهم كالعاده وهم يلوا صليبه ومينه واخدونا بس وكرام كبير الى كنيسه السيده لان ها ثلاث كنايس. فهذه الواحدة والاخري علي اسم الثالوث والاخري على "اسم القديسه باراسكافي وساقين هذه كنيسه السيده ايضاً بدل التريا اقرن وحش كبير بشعايب كثيرة وراسة مصنع وه الشموع
Let us return to the description of the three hundred houses in the valley. There are two churches there. The name of this place is Isai. Each house is surrounded by a garden whose fence consists of cherry trees, plum trees, and other trees. In the soil of the gardens, they cultivate cabbage, carrots, radishes, parsley, romaine lettuce, etc. The whole forest is surrounded by hedges on all sides and has owners. When we had finally come out of the forest and the narrow path, we traveled another mile, in all four miles on that day; we arrived in a large locality, fortified by a fortress, called Boguslav, and near there we crossed in boats a wide river called the Ros. After crossing the river, we were welcomed by the six priests of this city dressed in their vestments, with banners, the choirboys, by the people, and the soldiers carrying the war flag of the Hetman Zenobius, who loves Christ, a flag in pink where black stripes alternate with yellow stripes; it is adorned with a cross. When my lord the patriarch stepped onto land, all prostrated themselves before him; he kissed their cross and their icons as usual, and they kissed his cross and his right hand. They took us with great honors into a church dedicated to the Holy Virgin, for in this city there are three churches. That one is one, the second is dedicated to the Holy Trinity, and another to Saint Paraskeva. In this church of the Holy Virgin, they have hung, as a chandelier, the horn of a large wild animal with numerous branches, and its head, worked with art, carries candles.
واما الخثمان اخميل فكان نازل بجموعه خارج هذه المدينه فارسلوا له ين ديوم الارعة ضحوة حاء بخن انه ايه ليسلم علي سيدنا الطرك فخرجنا لاستقباله خارج منزونا لان هاهنا طريق القلعه وهيروا له مكان ها
Our arrival was announced to the Hetman Hmilnitzki, who was camped with his troops outside the city. On Wednesday morning, it was announced that he was coming to greet my lord the patriarch. We went out to meet him outside our dwelling situated on the road to the citadel, where an apartment had been prepared for him. XXVIII. — Interview with Hmilnitzki and the gifts that were offered to him.
ثم اقل حينئد من باب المدينه بجماعه كثير ما عرف احدا ان يفرقه منهم لان جميعهم كانوا بملابس حسنه واسلحه ثمينه واما هو فكان ياب حقيره قصيرة ومقلد بالعدة الغير تمينه فمعما نثار سيدنا الطرك من بيد نزل عن فرسة! مع بيه من معه وح" الى عنده وسجد له وقبل اطراف دياله اولا وتان ثم الصليب مع .يمينه فقبل سيدنا إانطرك_راسه اين عينيكم يا باكات الغضان والفلاخ واين عظمتكم وكبرياكم وكل واحد منكم ما هو بميمة احد بروكونيكوس من خدامه لكن بعدل وانصاف ربا اعطالا وهناا شىء تعجز عنه
He arrived through the city gate with a numerous retinue from which one could not have distinguished him, for all were dressed in beautiful clothes and carried expensive weapons. As for him, he was dressed in very poor clothes and girded with inexpensive weapons. When from afar he caught sight of my lord the patriarch, he dismounted from his horse as did those who accompanied him, headed toward him, and prostrated himself before him, kissing the hem of his garment, a first and a second time, then the cross as well as his right hand. My lord the patriarch kissed his head. Where are your eyes, O princes of Moldavia and Wallachia? Where is your greatness and your pride? None of you is worth a 'polkovnik' of those who are in his service; and yet in justice and equity, God has


[p. 655] {#po.26.p655}


[p. 656] {#po.26.p656}
الملوك لانه بالحال اخد كناف سيدنا اللطرك ومشى به خطوه خطو الى داخل_القعه وهو يبكي الي ان جلسوا على المايده وجلست البروكونيكات! معه فكنت تنظر يا هذا الى حسن كلامه ووداعته وخشوعه واتضاعه وبكلا لانه فرح بسيدنا البطرك كثير جد واحبه لغايه قايلًا المجد الله الذي مر علي قبل موتي بالاجتماع مع قدسك وتكلم معه" كثيراً في امور شت وكل شي أله اطاعه لان بيك الفلاخ كير قسطنطين مع اكابر الفلاح كانوا خايفين منه كثيراً ومترقبين مجيه اليهم بساك علي حين غفله سبب كان قد فعله متتي بيك حين كسرته للقزرق عساكره من القتل واليس وغير ذلك وطلبوا من سيدنا البطرك كثيراً ان يشفع عنده ان يصفح عنهم وبرسل له منهم مكتوب يطمن خواطرهم فاعطاه سواله وارسل لهم ما طلب وكذلك ستافانى بيك البعضان الجديد كان خايف منه كثيراً بسبب قتل انه ثيموتاوس وغير ابح فلوها البفضائليه مع القزق من القتل فصفح عنهم ايض وارسل له مكتوب ايض حواب مكاتيم له
given him what emperors could not have. Then taking my lord the patriarch by the shoulders, he walked at a pace with him until they had entered the citadel. While walking, he was weeping; finally, they sat down to eat. The 'polkovniks' sat with him. One could admire his kind language, his modesty, his humility, and his tears. He was very happy to see my lord the patriarch and he showed him all his sympathy by saying: 'I give thanks to God for having permitted me before dying to meet with your Holiness.' He spoke with him at length on different subjects with deference, and he answered every request: concerning Constantine, Prince of Wallachia, who feared him greatly, as well as his boyars, and who expected a sudden invasion of his armies because of the proceedings of Prince Matthew, when his troops defeated the Cossacks and massacred a part of them, and took another part into captivity, etc... Constantine and his boyars asked my lord the patriarch to intercede in their favor with him in order to obtain his pardon and with a letter from him to calm their hearts. The Hetman gave satisfaction to the patriarch and sent them what he had requested. Likewise, Stephen, the new Prince of Moldavia, feared him greatly because he had killed his son Timothy and because of other turpitudes that the Moldavians had committed with regard to
وسال عن امور كثير»؛ ثم اننا احضرنا له البديه في صحون مغطايه بمحارم كعادتهم وهى قطعه حجر ودم سيدنا المسيح من الجلجلة المقدسه ووعاآ ميرون مقدس وعلة صابون ممسك وصابون مطيب وصابون حلبى وعليه سكر نات ولادن وثمر ومشمش ورز ووعآ بن اي تبون لانه محب له وخيار القن وكان جالس مقابله وزيا وايضا طنفسه سداجه تمينه وخاس الناس عنده بيصاري غرامايكون هو من البروكويكن! عده عشربا وجميعهم محلوقين اللحا كعادتهم ومننى اسم القزق هو هذا اي محلوقين اللحا متفاونين الشوارب وهذا اخميل فهو رجل شيخ آلا انه مارك جد على بركة الله ولكنه هادي ناصت غين متجنب مزالناس ماشرا الساين الامور بنفسه قنوع بمأكوله ومشروبه وملبوسه مستسير بحسب سيرة عظيم الملوك فاسيلي المكدوني كما ييخر عنه التاريخ وكل من بينظرة بيتعجب منه قابلًا هذا هو اخميل بعد ان الذي صوته وذكر شاع في العالم جميعه كما اخبرونا ان فى بلاد الافرنج عملوا له اشعار ومدايح على سفراته وغزواته وفتوحاته فاذا كات صودته حفيرة الله معه هذا شني عظيم لان فاسيلي بيك الغضان كان قامه وجهامه ومنظر وهيسه بكلمه نافد بصيت في كل العالم باموال وغنايم عظيمه ولكن
the Cossacks by massacring them. And he forgave them too. He also sent him a letter in response to their letters. He also questioned him about many other things. Then we offered him gifts on trays covered with fabrics as usual, namely: a fragment of the stone of the Holy Calvary and of the Blood of Our Lord Jesus Christ, a vial of holy chrism, a box of musk soap, toilet soap, Aleppo soap, a box of rock candy, perfumes, dates, apricots, rice, a vase containing coffee beans, of which he is fond, cassia, and a prayer rug of great value. His vizier was opposite him, as well as his retinue, the 'grammaticos,' and ten of his 'polkovniks,' all having their beards shaved according to their custom, which explains the meaning of the word Cossack, which means one who has 'a shaved beard' and who is content to wear his mustache. Hmilnitzki is an elderly man, blessed by God, calm, silent, easy of access, occupying himself with his affairs, sober in food, drink, and clothing, and content with little like the great Emperor Basil the Macedonian, as history reports. Whoever sees him admires him, saying: 'Is this really Hmilnitzki, whose fame has spread throughout the whole world?' According to what has been reported to us, in the land of the Franks, they have composed verses and hymns of praise to celebrate his campaigns and his conquests. His appearance was rather modest, but God was with him, which is the essential thing. Basil, Prince of Moldavia, was of tall stature, had a severe look, presence, an imposing air; his word was listened to and renowned everywhere, thanks to his riches and immense spoils,


[p. 657] {#po.26.p657}


[p. 658] {#po.26.p658}
ما افاده هذا جميعه شيع ولكنه فى اول حريه وتانيًا وثالث ومرات كثيرة كان يولى هازياً اين اسمك يا اخميل اين صودتك وأين فعلك حت ان الله معك وهو الذى اقامك لتتقد شعبه الخاص من عبودية الامم كما انقد فى القديم موسى الاسرايليين من عوديه فرعون وذاك غرتهم في بحر الاحمسر وانت افنيتهم اي اللاه الانجاس بسيفك الابثر المجد لله الذي فعل بك هذه الامور العظام كلبا واما اذا كان ياتى احد ونشتكى له على المايده او بخاطه فكان يكلمه سر حيث لايسمع احداً وهذه عادته دايمً! واما جلوسه على المايده فانه جلس اسفل واجلس سيدنا البطرك في الصدر حسب الاكرام الواجب في المحفل ما هو مثل باكات البغضان والفلاخ هم في الصدر وربيس الكبنه اسفل ثم انهم احضروا للمايده صحون عرق وكانوا يشريوة المعالق وهو بعد سخن وما هو فكانوا هم يوضموا له في قدح فضه من عرق اخاص ثم يسقىي سيدنا الطرك اولا وشرب هو تان وكان ينزل عند كل واحد منا لاثنا كنا وقوف مقابلهة انظر الى هذه النفس الترابيه ادام الله وجودها ولا عنده: سقاه ولا من يخطبي اواعي مشروة رماكوله كما عاد الملوك بل "الحكام ثم احضروا على المايدة صحون قبشاني سمك مملح مطبوخ وغيرا شيء .سير ولا صحون فضه ولا تلورات فضه ولا معالق ولا غير ومع هذا فان عند كل واحد من خدام خدامه صناديق عديدة مملولا من صحون وكاسات ومعالق ودخاير اللاه الفضه والذهب ولكن ليس لهم جميعهم' اعتناء بذلك لكون انهم في السفر واما اذا كانوا في بيوتهم ومواطنهم فشي غير هذا
but all that was useless to him, for in the first, the second, and the third combat, as in the following ones, he was defeated and put to rout. But you, O Hmilnitzki, how different are your name, your appearance, and your acts! In truth, God is with you! It is He who raised you up to save His chosen people from the servitude of foreign nations, as in antiquity Moses saved the Israelites from the servitude of Pharaoh, by causing the army of the latter to perish in the waves of the Red Sea; you, you have annihilated the impure Poles by the edge of your sword; glory to God who through you has operated such great things! When someone came to complain to him at the table, or to speak to him, his habit was to answer him in a low voice, so that no one would hear what he was saying. At the table, he gave the first place to my lord the patriarch and placed himself after the patriarch, according to the honors required in a ceremony. He did not conduct himself like the princes of Moldavia and Wallachia who sat in the first rank and placed the high priest below them. At the table, they served cups of brandy that was still warm, which they drank with a spoon, and for Hmilnitzki, they presented him with a silver cup with a special brandy. He poured to drink first to my lord the patriarch, then he drank in his turn and toasted the health of each of us, while we remained standing in front of him. Admire this modesty; may God preserve it for him for a long time! He has no cupbearers, nor boys to cover the drinking vessels and the dishes, according to the habit of kings and even governors. They then brought to the table earthenware dishes, loaded with cooked salted fish and other dishes in small quantity, without silver dishes, nor silver plates or spoons: and yet each of his servants had chests full of plates, cups, spoons, and treasures of silver and gold won from the Poles, but no one cared about them, for they were in the middle of a military expedition. But when they are back in their country and in their homes, things happen differently.
وخين العص ودع سيدنا البطرك وركب في قاروصة التي راس واحد خيل لا غير وودعه لخارج باب القلعه ولا قوجيات ملوكية مزينه بالاثواب النمينه ولا بكثره عديده من الخيل الحسنه ومع هذا فمنده منهم الوف وسار بالحال فى ساعة :مطر عظيم ومضي الي حيث عسكره وعليه' يمرلق ابيض وانصرف بعد ان ارسل لنا خرجية الطريق واستعدر ثم مكتوب لاير بلاده بمأكول ومشروب ثم سخ خيل وقاروصات ثم مكاتيب لملك المصكوف ولويوضه بوتيبليا هذا ما خرى ْ
In the afternoon, he said his farewells to my lord the patriarch, and he accompanied him outside the gate of the citadel, then he left in his carriage with a single horse. He had no royal carriages, covered with expensive fabrics, nor harnessed with a large number of pretty horses, even though he had thousands of them. At that moment, a great rain occurred. He left to join his army dressed in a white cloak, after having sent us our travel expenses while apologizing for the little he was giving, and by joining to it letters of recommendation for his whole country, so that they would provide us with provisions; then he requisitioned horses and carriages; likewise, he had letters delivered to us for the Emperor of Moscow and for the Voivode of Putivl. That is what happened.
اعلم ان حكم بلاد اللاد كان ثلثة اقسم فلقسام: الواحد هذا الذي اخذه منهم وتملكه
XXIX. — Organization of the land of the Cossacks. Remark. — Formerly, Poland was divided into three parts: one is that which the Hetman Zenobius Hmilnitzki conquered and whose length as


[p. 659] {#po.26.p659}


[p. 660] {#po.26.p660}
الخثمان ذنوبيوس اخميل طوله شبن وعرضه شهر وهو معمور جميعه بالقلاع والحصون صفة الرمانه والقسم الثاني هو الباقي معهم الآن والقسم الثالث الذي هو الاوسط فانه اخريه للغايه واحرق بلاده وضياعه وقتل رجاله واكترهم صاروا يسرى للتطر وجعله بريه خاليّه اي سنار فيها بينه ودينهم مسافة كم يوم اعلم ان عند الان تمانية عشر بولكونيكوس! اي باشاوات وكل من منهم ضابط بلاد وحصون كثير التى لا تحصا اهلا ففيهم اربع خمسه تحت .يد كل واحد مهم خمسون ستون بازار وليم من العساكر المديونه ستون الف محارب وخمسون الف وارحون الف والاصضن منهم تحت يد كل واحد ثلاثون ارسون بازار ولبم من الصاكر ثلاثون_ الف عشرون الف والأوصا منهم تحت يد كل واحد؟ عشرون بازار ونازل فليم من الساكر عشرون الف ونازل
the width is equivalent to a month's march; it is cultivated and covered with citadels and forts like the inside of a pomegranate. The second part is that which remains to them now. The third is that which is in the middle, which was ravaged by the Hetman, by burning the cities and villages, by killing the inhabitants and taking most of them into captivity to the Tatars, and by transforming it into a desert; this part constitutes the border between them and him; it has a length of a few days' march. Remark. Currently, the land of the Hetman counts eighteen 'polkovniks' or pashas, and each of them governs a district and several forts; the population of these districts is innumerable. There are four or five of these districts where the government has under its authority from fifty to sixty towns, and as an army forty, fifty, and sixty thousand indebted (?) combatants. The smallest pasha has from thirty to forty towns and from twenty to thirty thousand soldiers, and the smallest of them has under his authority twenty towns or less and an army counting twenty thousand soldiers or less.
فتجتمع هذ العساكر جميعبا على اخميل حين السفر أكثر من خمسمايه الف قد تعلموا فنون اصناف حيل الحروب للفايه وظهر عنده الان نحو ماية الف جميعهم شباب شجمان فرسان الخيل والميدان لان هولاء الساكر اول كانوا رعيه وما ليم مرا فى الحروب بالكليه لكنم اتعلموا جايه جايه واما هولاء الشباب المذكورين باجمتهم فتعلموا الفروسيه والشجاعه ورمتي التفنك والسهام من صغرهم
At the moment of an expedition, the whole army gathers under the orders of Hmilnitzki to the number of more than five hundred thousand men perfectly instructed in the art and ruses of war. He currently has about one hundred thousand brave young men, consummate horsemen. These soldiers were at first simple citizens knowing nothing about the things of war, but they learned little by little. As for the young men, from their youth they have all learned the science of the horseman and the art of fighting, of firing gunshots, and of shooting arrows.
اعلم ان هذه الساكر جميعهم بغير علوفه ولكن بيزرعوا زراعاتهم علي قدر ما يريدوا وشتهوا واخيراً بيحصدوها وديدخلوها لبيوتهم ولا من ياخد علي العشرلا واحد ولا غير ذلك لكنهم معفايين من جميع ذلك وهكذا على هذا الحال جميع رعية بلاد القرق ولا بيعرفوا خرج ولا خراج ولا عشور ولكن اخميل مضمن ساير كمرك ارات بلاده من البازركان وخرج مآ العسل وماء الشعير والعرق بماية الف ديئنار فى السنه الكمركجه وهذه بتكفيه خرحيه في السنه كلها وغير هذا ما بياخد
Remark. All these troops are without pay. They cultivate their lands, as much as they want, then they harvest and bring their crops home. They are not subject to tithes and other taxes of that kind; they are exempt from them; this is the case for all the inhabitants of the land of the Cossacks; they know neither land tax nor tithes. Hmilnitzki collects customs duties at the borders of the country on merchants; the tax on beer, mead, and brandy brings in one hundred thousand dinars per year in customs duties, a sum that suffices for his annual expenses. That is all he collects.
فهذ اخار اخميل والقزق التي دكرناها علي التفصيل بالاجتهاد والتحرير وكثرة السوالات على التحتيق جميعها يتعب ونضب على التصديق ونا نما تبرت اللبلى وانا أكتب وما ابالي واما قلعة بوغوصلافى المذكور فهى حصينه جد بسوران وخندقان واحده داخل اخري
That is what concerns Hmilnitzki and the Cossacks, everything that we have reported in detail on their organization, and all these pieces of information I have collected and carefully recorded without sparing my efforts, questioning incessantly to verify their truth. How many vigils I have spent to write them and without looking at my fatigue! XXX. — Citadel of Boguslav. As for the citadel of Boguslav of which we have spoken, it is very strong, having two ramparts, two ditches, one inside the other, with


[p. 661] {#po.26.p661}


[p. 662] {#po.26.p662}
وابراجها كثيرة وطرنها القلي جميعه متطرف في اعلا الجيل وتحتها نهر المدينه المدكور وفيه الصخور العظام وها ايض سرايات كانت للا عاليه معظمه وريه كنيسه كات ليم ايض اعطاهم سيدنا اللطرك اجازه ان يكرسوها وصلوا بها لان القزق كانوا قد اخريوا. جميع كنايس اللاه للارض زعموا ليستاصلوا دكرهم وهذا السبب هجروا عمايرهم وسراياهم الملوكيه ايض ولا عداوة الا عداو الدين وجعلوهم ماوي للنجاسه ايض
numerous bastions. The southern part is entirely on the edge of a hill. At the foot of the citadel passes the river of the city, cluttered with very large rocks. In the city, there are high and large palaces that had belonged previously to the Poles. Near the citadel, there is a church that belonged to the Poles. My lord the patriarch authorized the inhabitants to consecrate it to pray there, for the Cossacks had destroyed and razed the Polish churches in the interior in order to erase even their memory. That is why the Cossacks had abandoned their constructions and their royal palaces, for there is no enmity like that of religion, and the
ثم اننا قمنا من بوغوصلافي نار الخميس حزيران واتفق طرقنا بين طابود. عساكر القزق واخميل وكانوا جميعهم قد رحلوا ثم ارسل الخثمان يستدعي سيدنا الطرك الى عنده ايض لانه اتعوق عن سق بسب ذلك ثم دخانا بين الساكر فكنت تنظرهم يا هذا الوف وربوات متبادرين ومتقاطرين لتقبيل .يمين سيدنا البطرك والصليب وهم ساجدين حتي ان خيل الفوجيه وقفوا وزعلنا ضجرنا منهم لكترتهم الي ان وصلنا لعند خيمة الختمان اخميل وهي صغير حقير فخرج لاقا لسيدنا الطرك ايض وسجد له فقرا عليه حينثد
Cossacks made them into impure refuges. We left Boguslav on Thursday, June 23. Our path passed through the camp of the Cossacks and of Hmilnitzki who were on their departure. The Hetman invited my lord the patriarch to his place, for he had postponed his expedition because of that. Then, we entered the ranks of the soldiers. One saw them by thousands and tens of thousands running in long files in order to kiss the hand of my lord the patriarch and the cross while prostrating themselves on the ground, so that the horses of the carriages were stopped; how we were inconvenienced and tired of their multitude.
افاشين الحرب والظفر ودعا له ولماكره ثم انه متك بأكتافه وادخله اللخيمته حينتد وف مكلف ما فيه طنافن ولكن بسط حقيرلا وكان "حالس يتغدا والمايده. موضوعه وغير صحن شمر مطبوخ لا غير ما كان ومع هذا فكنا ننظر الي غامان عساكره وهم يصطاديا من البحرات التي حوليم سمك لنفوسهم فانظر الي هذد القناعه ثم اسقانا عرق وقمنا +خرج معه ودعه :ايض ورحلنا
We finally arrived at the modest and small tent of the Hetman. The latter came out to meet my lord the patriarch, prostrated himself before him; the patriarch recited the prayers for war and victory for him, and prayed for him and for his army. Then the Hetman took him by the shoulders and introduced him into his tent. There were no Persian rugs, but low-priced Kurdish rugs. He was sitting and having lunch, and on the table that was set, there was only a plate of cooked fennel, while we saw the young soldiers of his army fishing for fish in the neighboring ponds for their own account. What frugality! Then the Hetman poured us a glass of brandy,
وجميع الشاكر ما ليم خيم ولكن بيصفوا الشجر حوليم وقضان صفة خيمه بيوضعوا عليهم 'يغمرلقاتهم لاحل المطر وهم قنوعين للغايه بارك الله فيهم ثم مشينا في هذا اليوم اربعة اميال كبار في ارض وطلا وربيع طوبل عيض الي ان اتينا المسآ الي قرية اسمها كوكاري كانت اول بحصار ولكن خرت في الاسفار ثم قمنا منها نهار الجمعه وعدينا علي قرتين كباز ومشينا ثلاث اميال وحنا الي بلده تبره ااسنها أترينوليس. الانها: الث مدن .بحصارات توقيل ما تصل: "اليا ننغلي الى. طابور وهو تلال تراب كثيره ومدخابا بين نحيلين تراب شيق "جد لا يدخله غيز واحد واحد لا غير وخرجوا اهلا لاقونا وهي قلعه كبيرة حصينه جد في راس جيل بسوران وخندقان
we stood up, and he went out with the patriarch, accompanied him, and we left. The army has no tents. They arrange trees all around them or assemble branches in the shape of a tent and cover them with their rain cloaks. They are content with little. May God bless them! We traveled that day four large miles, in a low, long, and wide plain, rich in pastures. We arrived toward evening at a village called Kokari, which was formerly fortified, but which was destroyed during the last wars. We left there on Friday, and we passed through two large villages; we walked three miles and arrived at a large city called Tripoli, for it is composed of three fortified cities. Before arriving there, one sees the camp composed of numerous mounds of earth and having its entrance between two hills, but so narrow that only one person can enter at a time. The population came out to meet us. There is a large, very strong citadel built on the summit of a mountain, with two ramparts and two ditches. Most of its houses are empty, for previously they


[p. 663] {#po.26.p663}


[p. 664] {#po.26.p664}
واكش بيوتها خاليه لانها كات ولا مركز اليبود وديوتهم الحسنه ودكاكينهم وخاناتهم خاليه ما هم ساكن
were inhabited by Jews. Their beautiful houses, their shops, and their Khans are today deserted.
ثم اخدونا الي كنيسه با علي اسم التجلي الالبي معظمه كبيرة واسعه حسنه فاقت في محاستها وكبرها وكثرة قمارها علي جميع كنايس بلاد القزق لاننا لما دخنا الها اندهثت عقولنا واندهلنا وهي عليه جد نبشتان شبابيك بلور كبار دايرها بقبه عاليه جد واسعة مدورة مشمنه صفة باب بلادنا مقطايه بتنكا تلمع وكذلك فوق حنية البيكل قبه اخري لطيفه وفوق الاب اخري ثلاث صلبان واما اليكل الالبي. فمعظم حدً واما الشمبلن بيحيى العقول من علولا وابتهاجه وتصورة وتدهيبه الا ان ليس اعتنا فى هذه اللاد فى عم الصلبوت مثل الروم لكنه لطيف واما صورة يوحنا والسيده حوله فواقغين. مفردين لا يظبر تحتهم دف ولكن شبه انسان كامل واما ابواب البيكل فمعظمات كبار مخرمات مدهبات ولا ب عن ايقونة منديل الصوزه تكون فوق الباب الملوكي لان هكذا عادتهم فى هذه اللاد وفوتها ايض ابقونة اليد كامل حالس على كرسي وهو لابن ماكو » متحف وتاج وحوله يوحنا المعمدان وأسيّده وطرس ومفاتيحة ورفقتة ' الخمسةه على يمينه واص بسيفه يعلي شماله ودفقته علي شماله لانهم في جميع قونسطاسات هذه البلاد ما بيصورهم الا علي هذه الطريقه ولا بد عن صورة بواص مقابل بطرس من الاثئي عشثر واما في وسط الخورسص ففيه داكه من دف بدرحتان .لله ,جوخ ابر كيز فوقها لغماس وقرا الانجيل وارض هذه الكنيسه بلاط حجر وجميعها معقودة يخشبالصنوير! فى بعضه بعض بعجيب الصناعه واما خارحها فداير ما يدورها رواقا لطيف جدا بدرابزين مخروط نه عاليه فو أكل: باب من ابوايا. الاثنان. عمرها سمي اليا ايحن كان :اسمه انود من الفتح الختماني أي من مدة تمان سنين من تاريخه وصلينا .ها في هذه ليل مولد يوحنا المعمدان وصباح الاورثرن
Then, they took us to a church dedicated to the Divine Transfiguration; it is very large, spacious, and beautiful, superior in its beauty, its size, and the number of its small bull's-eye windows to the other churches of the land of the Cossacks. When we entered it, we were amazed: it is very large with two rows of large windows all around, with a very high dome, spacious, round at the top, and the rest in an octagonal shape, like the domes of our country; it is covered with shiny tin. Likewise, above the arch of the sanctuary, there is another elegant dome, and above the door yet another with three crosses. The divine sanctuary is very large, and the symbol excites admiration by its elevation and its beauty, by its paintings and its gilding; one does not care in this country for the size of crucifixes as among the Byzantine Greeks, but one prefers them graceful. The images of Saint John and of the Holy Virgin are placed standing separately around the Crucified below these images. One does not see a wooden lintel, but something like a whole man. The doors of the sanctuary are very large, openwork, and gilded, and one always finds an icon painted on a veil above the royal door: it is their habit in the land of the Cossacks. Above this icon is the icon of Jesus Christ, seated on a throne, dressed in a precious 'sakkos,' wearing a crown on his head. Around him, to his right, are Saint John the Baptist, the Holy Virgin, Saint Peter with his keys, and his five companions; to his left are Saint Paul with his sword and his companions. On all the iconostases of this country, one paints apostles only in this way. One always sees the icon of Saint Paul opposite that of Saint Peter, among the twelve. In the middle of the choir, there is a circular wooden platform with two steps, covered with red cloth; it is on this platform that the deacon says the litanies and reads the Gospel. The floor of this church is paved with stone slabs. It is vaulted with pine beams inserted one into the other with wonderful art. On the outside, all around, there is a very beautiful peristyle with turned wooden balustrades. It has a high dome above each of its two doors. It was built after the conquest of the Hetman by the archon Samiyulbaga, called Banov, that is to say, eight years ago. On the eve of the Nativity of Saint
وقريا القاعة الثانيه وهي معظمه جد لطيقه حسنه حصينه للفايه وداخلبا سرايات ملوكيه تنعش خاطر ناظرهم من خارج قبل داخليم واما قبة القصر العالبه فوق باب لقلمه فلطيفه جد وعاليه وفوتها ايض فيه اخرى لزينة الناظرين وداير ما يدورها
John the Baptist, we celebrated vespers, and the next morning, matins. Near this church is the second citadel, very large, elegant, and well-fortified. Inside, there are royal palaces of a splendid appearance, the exterior of which delights the eyes of the visitor before he penetrates inside. As for the high dome of the palace, which is above the gate of the citadel, it is very high and graceful: further on, there is another ravishing dome, surrounded by superb balustrades: the people who


[p. 665] {#po.26.p665}


[p. 666] {#po.26.p666}
درابزين عجيب يظهن من فيه من مير يوم وهي احسن من عمارة كالينوسكه بكثير ومقابل! هذ القلعه بيوت اللاد والبهود ودكاكينهم وخاناتهم الحسنات الممهجورات سالنا باناكي الهودي المتنصس في البغضان ماذا فعل الختمان اخميل مع الببود في اللاه فقال انه فعل معهم من الشر والقتل اشن واعظم مما فمل اسباسيانوس في القديم فضحكنا لذلك اخرونا فى هثلا البلاد انه لما ظهر اخميل وطبى الارض من عدة الوف. اليبود ولما حا الي هذه البلاد اجتمعوا جميع الذين تبقوا من الهود واغنياهم؟ واكابرهم ومضوا الي قلعه اسمها ضولجينى بارزاقهم واموالبم وتحصنوا "بها بالمدافع4 والبارود والزخيره فجااوا القرق وحاصروهم بها وفتحوها بالسيف ودخلوا الها وقتلوا جميع من كان با بالعصي والحطب لا بالسيوف وكانوا نحو من عشرون الف نسمه حتي والاطفال في بطون الحالي كان يخرجوهم برباح ويقتلوهم وعلي هذا الحال افنوهم بالجمله وذكروا لنا انهم قتلوا فى مدينه واحد اسما"... سعون الف يهودي لان الكفر ما كان يكفييم ظلمهم ولكنيم كانوا ايزنون بنساهم وناتهم فلإجل ذلك غضب الله علهم وعلي اللا الدين حكموهم وحازوا وغنموا اموالهم ودخايرهم وكانوا يتقاسموا الدهب الذي في البراميل بالكيل وباعوا غالى الاتمان بالبوان كل صحن فضه مرش او كاس او شمعدان او مبخا او غير ذلك شىء! لا يعرفوا قيمته وكانوا الكفرة اليبود من خثهم لما تظردا انهم ماخودين أرموا في اللبل جميع دهيهم وفضتهم وصيفتهم وتجملاتم في بحرة المآ الجاربه حول القلعه فاخرجوا القرق جميعه بحسن فكرتهم وبصيرتهم وخاب امل الكفرة وظنهم
are there can be seen from the distance of a day's march. It is much prettier than the construction of Kalinovski. Opposite this citadel, one sees the Jewish and Polish houses; their shops and their pretty Khans are abandoned. Having questioned the Jew Lenachi, who had taken refuge in Moldavia, on the way the Hetman Hmilnitzki had treated the Polish Jews, he replied that by massacring them and tyrannizing them, he had done worse to them than Vespasian in antiquity. We began to laugh at his answer. We were told, in this country, that when Hmilnitzki appeared and rid the earth of a few thousand Jews, and when he had arrived in this place, those of the rich and notable Jews who remained gathered and took refuge in the citadel called Tulchin, with their provisions and their goods; they fortified themselves there with cannons, powder, and victuals. The Cossacks came to besiege it and conquered it by the sword; they entered it and killed all those they found there, with blows of sticks and logs, not with sword blows; they were to the number of about twenty thousand; they even killed the children in the wombs of pregnant women; they took them out with the lance. In this way, they exterminated them. We were told that in a single city, called... O..., they had massacred seventy thousand Jews, for these infidels had not been content to tyrannize the Cossacks, but they were debauching their wives and daughters. That is why God became angry against them and against the Poles, who had given them authority. The Cossacks took and looted their goods and their treasures; they divided by the bushel the gold they found in barrels and sold precious objects at a low price: they sold for a piaster a silver plate or cup, a candlestick, a censer, or other similar objects, because they were ignorant of their value. When they saw themselves caught, these infidel Jews threw by malice during the night their gold, their silver, their jewelry, and luxury objects into the pond that was around the citadel; but the Cossacks retrieved them thanks to their ingenuity, and the hope of the infidels was disappointed.
ومقابل كنيسه المدكوره في القلعه الاخري كنيسه معظمه على اسم مار تكولا واما نهر يبروس العظيم فبو جاري بقرب هذه المدينه وفيه هاهنا بيعملوا مراكب التي تسير في
Opposite the aforementioned church, in the other citadel, there is a large church dedicated to Saint Nicholas. A great river, the Dnieper, flows near this city; ships are built there for navigation on the Black Sea.
بحر الاسود ثم اننا خرحنا منها صباح السبت ع حزيران ومشينا ميل واحد واتنا الي بازار اخر اسمه اويوخونا” بحمار عالي ايض وبه كنيستان حضرنا في احدهم قداس المعمدان وتفرجنا علي البناير اي البيع والشرآ الني يصير في عيده من السنه للسنه والكثيستان فعلى اسم القيامه ومار ميخاييل
We left the city on Saturday morning, June 24th; we traveled one mile and arrived at another town called Obukhiv, which was also fortified and possessed two churches. In one of them, we attended the feast of Saint John the Baptist. We visited the "panayir," which is the fair held every year on the occasion of this feast. The two churches are dedicated, one to the Resurrection and the other to Saint Michael.


[p. 667] {#po.26.p667}


[p. 668] {#po.26.p668}
ثم رحلنا منها ومشينا ميل اخر وحيًا الى حصار خراب فيه كنيسه على اسم مار تقولا ومشينا ميل ثالك وجينا إلي ضيعه اخري اسمبها خاموكا وقريها نر عظيم عيض وكان في طريقنا هذا اليوم شجر الارز كثير واما سياحات الساتين والاراضي فجميعه كان صفصاف لائه كثير جدا في هذه الارض مع صفصاف رومي ايض ومطفور حوله قضبان السياج ثم مشيئا ميلا رابع مشينا فلاقانا صوتنيكس ومعه خمسون فارس ووصانا الي البلد المسما فاسيليكو وتحقيق انه اسم علي مسمآ لانها بلد كبيرة قوي وما هي واحد ولكن ثلاثة مدن كبار بقلاع وحصارات داخل بعضيم بعض علي راس جبل حصين جداً وكل واحده داخل الاخري ولكن جمعم خاليات لانه انا طاعون فافنا سكانهم من مدة سنتان ثم لاقونا الكبنه وبقية الشعب بالناحق الي خارجيم وصعدوا نا الي اعلاهم واخدونا الي كنيه معظمه داخل القلعه الثلثه علي اسم القديسين انطونيوس وتاودوسيوس المعظمين اي قديين بلاد القزق لان اول من اظهر عندهم السيرةٍ النسكيه الملايكيه اي قلالي ومغاير المتوحدين وانشا الرهبان والاديره هم هولاي القديسين وهم معظمين عندهم كثير وهذا الكنيسه فهي حسنه عليه وقونسطاسها صفة قوسطاس الروم عظيم ولكن ايقونه السيده بها كبير معظمه بتحير العقول لاننا ما نظرنا مثلها ولا عدنا نظرنا اصلا لانها ناطقه لحسن تصويرها واما توبا فهو مخمل احمر العادة غامق شفاف ما نظرنا مثل صناعته قط لان الارضيه غامق والطلات شفاف حسب طى المخمل وما الجيراز الدهب الذي فوق جيينها ونازك لا تحني آلا نانه نازله متحرك_ولنا وجا وفمها: يحي التول الحنته مااهى محتاجه ًا للنطق سلام الله عليها واما السيد وهو فى حطنها عجيب جد للغايه لانه كمن يتكلم وكما ذكرنا اني نظرت كثير من بلاد الروم وجايه الي هاهنا ثم من هامنا الي المصكوف فلم انظر لبا شبيه ولا اخت قطما لان مصتّرين القزق استرقوا ,محاسن الصور والوجوا والوان الاتواب من مصورين افرنج اللاه الاستادين وهم الان بيدوروا ايقونات الارتودكسيين فلبذا السبب هم معلمين ماهرين وليم اختراعات كليه في تصوين وجو الثان علي التحقيق كما نظرنا صورة تاوفاني بطرك اورشليم وغيرة
Afterward, we departed and, after a mile, we arrived at a ruined fort where there is a church dedicated to Saint Nicholas. We traveled a third mile and arrived at another village called Khamoka, near which there is a very large and wide river. That day, we encountered many cedars on our path. The hedges of the gardens and fields are made of willows, which abound in these places; the soil also produces the Greek willow, around which the branches of the bushes grow. After a fourth mile, a "sotnik" came to meet us with a troop of fifty horsemen. We arrived at the city called Vasilkov, and truly this name suited it, for it is a very large city. It is not alone, but is composed of three large cities with a citadel and forts; these three cities interpenetrate, and they are situated on the summit of a fortified mountain. All are deserted because of the plague that had perished their inhabitants two years prior. The remaining priests and inhabitants came out to meet us with banners, well outside the locality. They made us go all the way up and took us to a large church situated inside the third citadel, dedicated to the illustrious saints Anthony and Theodosius, who are the saints of the Cossack land, for they were the first among them to lead the ascetic and angelic life in cells and caves, as solitaries, and who founded monasteries for monks; these saints are held in very great veneration among them. This church is beautiful and tall. The iconostasis is grandiose like those of the Greeks. The great icon of the Holy Virgin is a marvel such as we have not yet seen the likes of. The Virgin seems to speak thanks to the perfection of the painting, and is so beautiful that one cannot describe her. As for the Virgin's garment, it is, according to custom, of dark red velvet, transparent; it is a work such as we have never seen, for the background of the painting is dark and the protruding parts are light, according to the folds of the velvet. As for the golden "djiraz" that is above her forehead and falls from it, one would say it descends and moves. The face and mouth surprise by their beauty; to this, only speech is missing: the peace of God be with her! Our Lord is in her arms; he is wonderful, one would say he is speaking. As we have said, we have seen many paintings in the Cossack land and on this side, up to here, then up to Moscow, but we have never seen the likes of these. The Cossack painters have stolen the beauty of portraits and the colors of draperies from the masters of European painting, from the Poles, and now they paint Orthodox icons. In their turn, they have become skillful masters and have truly created perfect works, by the expression of the human face, as we have noticed for example in the portrait of Patriarch Theophanes of Jerusalem and others.
اخبرنا ارشيمندرتي هذا الدير المعظم ان له الان من الاوقاف ثلاثون بازار اي بلد
The archimandrite of this great monastery informed us that his monastery now possesses as mortmain property thirty towns, large villages


[p. 669] {#po.26.p669}


[p. 670] {#po.26.p670}
بير معمور من الذي ذكرناهم وارسماية قريه عامرلا حتى وفى بلاد اللاه للان انهم بيحيوا جد وبيعتقدوا به كثير ثم انهم نزلونا في امطوش دير بجارسكه اي الدير الذي على اسم ناح اليّده خارج كيوف المشهور صيته فى كل العالم وهو فض بلاد القزق كما عدنا نظرنا لان هذا اللد المدكور جميعه مع ماية اخري مثله هم وقف على الدير المدكور من قديم وسبب هذا الدير جري ما جري على " اللاه من شق قسوس الايسوعيه لابل اليزيديه الذين ارادوا ان ياخدوه من الارتودكسيين وهو كان سب لزوالهم واضحلليم الي الغايه وهذه المدينه كنيستين اخري على اسم دخول السيدةٌ للبيكل ومار نقولا
prosperous ones of those we have mentioned, plus four hundred prosperous villages, currently situated in Poland, for this monastery is greatly venerated and one has great confidence in it. We were lodged in the procuracy of the monastery called Pecherska, dedicated to the Assumption of the Holy Virgin, outside Kiev, and which is known throughout the world and which is, as is known, the glory of the Cossack land. This city and a hundred others like it are legacies made, since ancient times, to this monastery. It is because of this monastery that all the evils caused by the Jesuit Fathers, what am I saying, the Yezidis, fell upon Poland. These Fathers wanted to take it from the Orthodox, and that was the cause of their ruin and destruction. In this city, there are also two churches dedicated to the Presentation of the Virgin in the Temple and to Saint Nicholas.
ثم اننا صلينا في الكنيسة المدكوره عشية ليل الاحد السادس بعد المنصرا وكذلك حضرنا الاورترن باكر ثم القداس
We celebrated vespers in this church on the eve of the sixth Sunday after Pentecost. Likewise, we attended matins the following morning, then mass.
وخرجنا الي بستان هذه الكنيسه بقريها وفيه شجر الكراز وقلب الطير كثير ثم شجر الجوز وكرم عنب لاننا كنا ما عدنا نظرناهم من الغضان ثم وسداب وضتور افرنجي ورى غامق
We entered the garden next to the church, where there were cherry trees, plum trees, walnut trees, and vines; we had not seen them since our departure from Moldavia, then street and European gillyflower, dark pink. XXXI. — The Pecherska Monastery.
ثم اصبحنا نهار الاثنين مشينا خمسة اميال كبار والصوتنيكس المدكور وجماعته بالبيرق يودعونا ثم اتنا في طرقات عسرلا ضيقه وحرش كثير الي قرب بحر مآ خالستو وطواحين وقف علي الدير المدكور ومن قبل ما نصل لباهنا نظرنا قابه تلمع من بعيد مع قاب كنيسة اجيآ صوفيا
On Monday, we rose early and traveled five long miles, accompanied by the "sotnik" and his troop with the flag, and they bade us farewell. After that, we passed through difficult paths and numerous forests, until near a "helesten" pond where there were mills, pious legacies made to the monastery. Before arriving there, we saw its domes shining from afar, with those of the church of Saint Sophia.
ولما خرجنا لطلعة الجبل اتا لاقا سيدنا الطرك رئيس دير المدكور وهم سمو ارشيمندريتي وهذه عادة رئيسا ديارة هذا البلاد الي المصكوف ان الا يسموهم لا ارشيمندريتي وكان معه اسقف عنده مع رهبان وركبوا فى قوحجية للدير ملوكيه مطليه بالدهب وداخلبا مخمل احمر جميعه ومضوا بنا نحو الدير ثم دخنا فيها بين بساتين لا تعد ولا تحصا الوف فيهم شجر الجوز وشجر التوت وكروم عنب كثير وكل بستان وبيوته داخله وجمعهم نحو ارعة خمسه الاف بيت مع ارعة خمسة الاف بستان والجميع وقف على الدير المدكور
When we took the steep path of the hill, the hegumen of the monastery, who is called archimandrite, came out to meet my lord the patriarch (it is the custom of the hegumens of monasteries up to Moscow, who are also called archimandrites), accompanied by a bishop who was staying with him and his monks. They made my lord the patriarch get into the monastery's carriage, a royal, gilded carriage, lined entirely with red velvet, and we left for the monastery. We passed through countless orchards planted with walnut trees, mulberry trees, and vines. Each garden also had houses, rising to the number of four to five thousand; there were four to five thousand gardens, the whole belonging as pious legacies to the said monastery.
ثم وصلنا الي مدينه سور وخندق وساين غزيرل ثم دخانا في طريق سلطاني
We arrived in a large city with a rampart, a moat, and numerous gardens, then advancing on the great imperial road, we


[p. 671] {#po.26.p671}


[p. 672] {#po.26.p672}
