Its iron clapper weighs about a quintal and a half of Aleppo; to set it in motion twelve young men were barely enough; no one was capable of setting the clapper in motion from the inside and could not reach its edge because of its width. When it was rung, our ears were deafened by the power of its resonance and its hum. I spoke to my companion by shouting at the top of my lungs, without being heard by him; it was to the point that this solidly timbered bell tower, higher and larger than all the bell towers we had seen, swayed and trembled; however, the sound of the bells of the Pechersk convent is more piercing and sharper, while the sound of these is duller and more nasal; one would say a
ودخانا للقداس في الكنيسه السعّده اجيا صوفيا التانيه لانها اسم علي مسما كما شاهدنا عياناً وسندكر وصفها في محله كما هي وفىي حين اكسيون استين قرعو ايض ثم خرجنا من القداس للطراباذا ثم وعاودنا عثيه أي ليلة الاحد السايع بعد العنصرة للاسبارنون وعملوا ليتين في النرتكس البراني وقال الشماس خاص يا الله شعبك من غير اغرنيه مم القداس: فى القد صفة مدينة كيوف وكيف امنوا القزق وابتنوا الكنايس والديورة!
bell of Homs. We entered the venerable church of Saint Sophia to attend mass there. It well deserves to be called the second church of Saint Sophia as we were able to see with our own eyes. Its description will be given later. At the 'Axion', the bells were rung again. We left the mass and went to sit at the table. In the evening we returned to the church for vespers; it was the eve of the seventh Sunday after Pentecost. The 'litia' was performed without vigil, in the 'exonarthex'. The deacon said: 'Save, O God, your people.' The next morning mass was celebrated. XL. — Description of the city of Kiev. How the Cossacks were converted to Christianity and built churches


[p. 706] {#po.26.p706}
اعلم ان مدينه كيوف القديمة كانت هاهنا وعد للان ابوابها واتار سوارها التراب مخادتها ظاهرد وعد باقي بها باب عظيم وبرج من الحجر يسمونه باب الدهب لانه كان عطلى بالدهب احرقود التطر اخيرا لما كدوها واحرقوها كانت معظمة كبيرلا جد وكان دير مجارسكه خارج سودها وكانت هذه كنيسة تجا صوفيا في وسطيا مع دين مار ميخاييل الذي قبته مطليه بالدهب للان مقابلبا وعدة كنايسن معظمات كثيرة حوليم لانها هي كانت في القديم قاعدة مملكة هذ البلاد كما اخبرونا هم لانه لما اشرق نور الايمان بالمسيح فى زمن باسيل الملك المكدونى بتاريخ سنة
and convents. Remark. The ancient city of Kiev was in this same place, and to this day, one can still see its gates and the traces of its earthen ramparts and its ditches. || there still subsists a large gate and a stone tower which is called the 'Golden Gate', because it was gilded. In these latter times the Tatars had burned it, after having surprised and set fire to the city. This city was very large and the Pechersk convent was outside its ramparts. The church of Saint Sophia was in the center of the city, as well as the convent of Saint Michael which is situated opposite and whose dome is still gilded to this day. All around, there is a large number of churches, for this city was formerly the capital of the kingdom, as we were told. When the light of the faith in Jesus Christ began to shine under the reign of Basil the Macedonian, in 651, that is to say from his time until


[p. 707] {#po.26.p707}
احدي وخسين وتتمايه اي من زمانه الى يونا هذا كما هو مورخ علي ابواب هذه الكنايس والديوره وتزوج فلاديميرس ملك الروس اخت باسيل الملك المسملا أويخا بعد ان انا معها مطارته واساققة واعمدوا الملك وساين اهل بلاده وكانوا امه عظيمه كما تيضر التواريخ لا ينتمون الي شريعه ولا يعتقدون ديانه وابتنت حيشيد الملكه كنايس كثيرة وديوره عندهم انوا البناوون! من القسطنطيئه استادون ولبذا السبب الكتابات جميعها روميه وكات حينيد القبايل التي حول بلاد كيوف كفر« بغير ايمان اي اللاد والمصكوف والتطر وغيرهم وكانوا يحاربوها دايا وكات هي تفلبهم الي ان اشرق منها الي عندهم نور الايمان بالسيح وامنوا خلاف التطر ٍ وكان مطران هذه كيوف في ذلك الزمان يحكم علي بلاد المصكوف ايض الي مضي ستون من تاريخ لما جا البطريرك كير ارميا القسطنطيني حينيد جعل ريس اساقفة المصكوف بطرك بداته يحكم ولا يحكم عليه لان هذا البلاد جميعها هم خاضعين للبطرك القسطنطينى ونوءوا باسمه فى الاحيان قايلين لان من القسطنطينيه اشرق لنا نور الايمان بالمسيح ومنها تسلمنا الطقوس والقسطنطيني دايماً بيرسل لهم اكسرخن اي الي هذ بلاد القزق ويعطولا حسنه وله اطلاع على ديودتهم كما اخبرونا ونظرنا عند ارشيمندرتى دير بجارسكه ادطاتيكونات قديمه من بطاركة القسطلطينيه السلفين من نحو خسماية علي رق وبضمونم انه دير براسه وكذلك رآينا عنده من المرحوم تاارفاني بطرك اورشليم ومن باييسيوس هذا بعده وكتب له ايض اصطاتيكون بلسانهم وعلم ليم سيدنا البطرك عليه بخط وختمه مضمونه باستقامة الارشيمندرت وانه دير براسه وغير ذلك ولكن هولاء روسآ الدياره فيهم علماة فقبات فضيحا” متمنطقين فلاسفه وليم سنوالات عميقه ًا انهم لا يسموا القسطنطيني ابكومانيكوس اي بطرك السكونه ولكن ارشيابيسكوس لاغير ولهم بذلك مباحته كثيره وشهادات غزيره حتي انهم حيرونا عظيم الحيره ولبم اعتقاد كلي هم وهذه البلاد جميعها الي المصكوف ان بطرك انطاكيه هو صاحب الح والريط رهو خليفة بطرس الرسول وله وحدة اولا خول السيد المسيح الحل والربط فى السما والارض وهو اقدم البطاركة وكانوا ياخدوا منه اوراق السيخوروخاتي اي الاستغقار بامانه كبيره واعتقاد كلى
our days, as one can see by the dates engraved on the gates of these churches and these convents, Vladimir, emperor of the Russians, married Olga, the sister of the emperor Basil. When she had brought her metropolitan and her bishops, they baptized the emperor and his whole nation. It was, as the historians report, a great people, who were not attached to the divine law, and did not profess any religious belief. Then the empress had many churches and convents built by master masons from Constantinople, which is why all the inscriptions are in Greek. The peoples who then lived around Kiev were pagans without faith: the Poles, the Muscovites, the Tatars and others, always making war on this city which defeated them incessantly, until the day when the light of the faith in Jesus Christ rose for them, with the exception of the Tatars. At that time, the metropolitan of Kiev was also that of the country of the Muscovites, and this lasted for another sixty years. When the patriarch of Constantinople, Kyr Jeremiah, arrived in this country, he raised the archbishop of the Muscovites to the rank of independent patriarch, for until then all this country depended on the patriarch of Constantinople. The inhabitants exalted his name, saying: 'It is from Constantinople that the light of the faith in Jesus Christ came to us and it is from there that we received the rites.' The patriarch of Constantinople always sent them an exarch, that is to say to the country of the Cossacks, and he was given an alms; he had the supervision of their convents as we were told. We saw at the archimandrite's of the Pechersk convent ancient 'istatikon' on parchment emanating from the ancient patriarchs of Constantinople and dating back about five hundred years; these documents attest to the independence of the convent. Likewise, we saw at his place documents from the deceased patriarchs Theophanes of Jerusalem and Paisios. The patriarch (Macarius) wrote them a 'cistatikon' in their language and my lord the patriarch affixed his own signature and seal to it, confirming the maintenance of his dignity as archimandrite, the independence of his convent, and other things besides. These hegumens are for the most part versed in science, law, logic, and philosophy: they raise profound questions. They do not give the title of 'ecumenical' to the patriarch of Constantinople, or patriarch of the inhabited earth, but they limit themselves to naming him archbishop, bringing many proofs and testimonies to the point that they greatly astonished us. They believe — and this as far as Moscow — that the patriarch of Antioch has the power to loose and to bind and that he is the vicar of Saint Peter to whom in the first place Our Lord Jesus Christ granted the power to absolve and to suspend in heaven and on earth. For them the patriarch of Antioch is the most ancient of the patriarchs and it is from him that one received with confidence and entire faith sheets of absolution.


[p. 708] {#po.26.p708}


[p. 709] {#po.26.p709}
وعلى هذا المنوال ساورد خبر لطيف اتا محله هاهنا وذلك انه جا الى عند سيدنا الطرك في هذه بلدة كيوف التي سندكرها كاهن مكلف رومي الجنس مقيم في مدينة يريد في الفرانسه واجتمع به بشوق كبير هذا حا الان الجي من عند ملكة سفاتضا الملة العظيمه وهى بنت بكر الى عند الختمان اخميل لانها كانت قدر ارسات له الجيان اتنان غير هذا من زمان ولما كانت بلادها هي سنار بلاد اللا عرفوهم ومسكوهم فارسات حينيد هذا الكاهن الي القسطنطينة ومنها اتي الي بلاد القزق الي عند اخميل برساله منها خطابً تمدح افعاله وتشكر اعماله وما فعله مع اعداها اللاند لانهم كما ذكرنا سابقاً كانوا تقد استحودوا علي كثير من بلادها وحكمبا قايله له تكون تعلم يقبن انى قد از لك جانب بلادي ستون الف محارب معونة لك لكي تافر باعداي. للغايه وكذلك بعد ان اجتمع هذا الكاهن مع الختمان حبز معي الجي من عند بمكاتيب واب لها م مضي الكاهن المدكور صحبة آلجى اخميل الى عند ملك المصكوف بربالة منها له ايض كذلك لان سنار بلادها من سنار المصكوف قريب وينها وبين الملك محبه عظيمه وفي بلادها رعية مصكوف كثير وانا اقول حاشيه من تكون انت .يا اخميل يا لابن الجاروخ كما قالوا اعداك اللاه حتى ترسل لك الملوك والملكات الالجيه والهدايا العظيمه المجد لله وحده الذي اقامك واخضع اعداك تحت قدامك ١
XLI. — Baba Elijah and the French philosopher. On this subject, I want to cite a pretty story, because it is in its place here. While we were in Kiev, my lord the patriarch received the visit of a distinguished priest, Greek by origin, living in the city of Paris, in France, and who was very eager to see him. || He had just arrived as ambassador of the queen of the great country of Sweden, the virgin queen, to the hetman Khmelnytsky, to whom she had sent, a long time ago, two other ambassadors. As his country was bordering on Poland, the Poles recognized them and arrested them. Then, she sent this priest to Constantinople and, from there, he came to the country of the Cossacks, to Khmelnytsky, with an autograph letter where she glorified his acts and praised his exploits and his conduct with regard to his enemies the Poles, for the latter, as we have reported previously, had conquered a large part of his country and his kingdom. She wrote to him: 'Know that I have equipped in my country sixty thousand combatants as reinforcements for you, so that you may completely defeat my enemies.' After that, this priest had an interview with the hetman Khmelnytsky, who attached to him an ambassador of his own carrying his written reply to the queen. This priest went in the company of Khmelnytsky's ambassador to the emperor of Moscow with a letter from the queen, addressed to him, for the border of Sweden is adjacent to that of Muscovy and there existed a great friendship between the emperor and the queen, the country of the latter containing many Muscovite subjects. I, for my part, add as a remark: 'Who are you then, Khmelnytsky, shod in heavy peasant shoes, as your enemies the Poles said, for kings and queens to send you ambassadors and rich gifts? Glory to God who raised you up and humbled your enemies at your feet!'


[p. 710] {#po.26.p710}
نعاود اخبرنا هذا الكاهن المسما بابا ايليا قايلًا انه خرج فى هذا الزمان فى بلاد الفرانسه رجل عالم فيلسوف للغايه مدهبه لوطروس وانقادت اليه خلايق كثيره هذا ظهر يشنع علي البايا باموراً كثيره منها انه ادسل اليه رساله لروميه قايلًا له سوال كان ملك عظيم المان وكان له خمسة نين وكان يحبهم جميعيم بالسوبة ثم انه قسم ملكه ينهم بالسوبة وكذلك صار فالواحد منهم انشق وزاغ عنهم وصار براي نفسه واما الارعه فقيوا علي محتهم لبعضهم بعض ووصية ابيهم فاى منهم بطاع الواحد الذي خرج عن اخوته ام الارعة الذين بقيوا علي وفاتهم ثم انه دد الجواب قابلًا ان الاررسة بطاركه والبابا خامسهم صاروا مند عبد بطرس الرسول وقية الرسل ومن المجامع المقدسة وبقيوا شي واحد كما هو معلوم زماناً طويل على الايمان المستقيم اخيراً انحرف الابا عنم اي انفصل عن الجبد او الكف الاصبع الواحدد فالواجب الاطاعة للاريعه لا لذلك
Let us return to our subject. This priest called Baba Elijah told us that in that time there appeared in France an educated man, a great philosopher, a follower of Luther; he had rallied around him many followers, he raised many difficulties for the Pope. Among others, he had sent a letter to Rome where he asked the Pope the following question: 'There was once a great king who had five sons whom he loved all equally. Then, he divided his kingdom among them in equal parts and it was so. But, one of them separated and departed from the others and he wanted to act as he pleased. The other four continued to love one another according to the last recommendations of their father. Which of these sons must be obeyed? The one who separated from his brothers or the other four who kept their commitments?' Then, the philosopher gave this answer: 'The four patriarchs and the pope who is the fifth among them remained united for a long time in the true faith since the time of the apostle Saint Peter, of the other apostles, and since the holy councils, as is known; in the end the pope separated from them, that is to say that a single finger separated from the body or the palm of the hand. Obedience is therefore due to the four and not to that one.'


[p. 711] {#po.26.p711}
قال فلما سمع اليا هذه الرساله لم يمكن احدً ان يرد له جواب اصلًا ومن كثر غيضه! ارسل للحين لملك الفرانسه بامرة ان يقتل الرجل المدكور ليلا تشيع مقالته. هذه في العالم وسجس الكنيسه علي زعمه فرد له الجواب الملك المدكور قابلًا الي لست قادر على ذلك واحرك على شر ساكن اذ كان داخل مملكتى مايتى الف بيت ينتقدون الآن على هنا الددعب وجميعهم. يحبونه باعداتي. الننين_حول بلادي كثرين غير الذين عندي وهم طايفة الانكلاز وفلمند وملة سفاتصا وغير ذلك واما قدسك في رومية لا حولك ولا عندك الا رومانيه فما لى قولا على قتله
When the Pope had heard the reading of this letter, as there was no one to answer it, he became very angry and immediately sent to the king of France the order to put this man to death so that he would not propagate this doctrine in the world and would not disturb the Church by his opinion. The king gave him this answer: 'I cannot do that, nor provoke troubles; in my kingdom there are two hundred thousand families who profess this doctrine and all love it. The enemies who surround my country are numerous, not counting those from within: the English, the Flemish, the Swedes and others still. Your Holiness in Rome is only surrounded by Romans. I, I cannot put him to death.'


[p. 712] {#po.26.p712}
وقال الفيلسوف المدكور جواب تانى اذا كان اليابا مدعى انه خليفة بطرس الرسول فلأولي هذه الدعوي بطرك انطاكيه لان بطرس الرسول اول ما صار بطر بها وحضي بكرام كلي با واما رونية فانه صلب ها
The mentioned philosopher gave this second answer: 'The Pope claims to be the successor of Saint Peter, but this right belongs first to the patriarch of Antioch, because Peter was its first patriarch and he was honored there while in Rome he was crucified.'
ثم اخبرنا لكاهن المدكور ان فى ساير بلاد الافرنجيه بحبون بطرك انطاكيه فلم به اعتقاد كالى بخلاف غير ثم الاسكندراني واما القسطنطينى والاورشليمي الواحد لاجل حلم الان يمقتود والآخر لاجل مشاوزته للافرنج في اورشليم بيبفضود
Then, this priest informed us that in the rest of France they love the patriarch of Antioch and that they believe in him to the exclusion of others, then comes that of Alexandria; as for those of Constantinople and Jerusalem, the French hate the one because of their current situation and they detest the other because of his wickedness toward the Franks who are in Jerusalem. XLII. — Old Kiev. The church of Saint Sophia.
نعاود لما كنا من اخبار كيوف وآجيا صوفيا واما كنيسة اجيا صوفيا المدكورة نبي علي تكوين اجيا صوفيا الاصليه المشهوره وعلي صفة قناطرها ودايرها وسطحها ولكي تلك لا يمكن عقل بشري ان يحصرها لكثرة فنون الوان رخامها وتكوين تفائين عمادتّ وهندامها وكثرة عمدها وتشامخا وعلو قابها واتساعها وكثرةة رواقاتها ومخادعها تلك اسم وفلًا وهذه اسم وصفة وهي مريعة البناة قبو كلها بحجر وقرميد وكلس جميعها داخلبا وخارجها لكن من نرتكسبا الغربي خراب بقدر نصنبا الباقي ذكروا ان التطر من قديم اخريوه واحرقوا واقامت خاب نحو ماية سنه ماوت للبهايم والدواب ثم عمرت واخريوها الفونباطي اي الروس الدين تبعوا البايا وقلعوا كل بلاطها وفيفستها ووضعوها في كنايسم لانهم اخبرونا ان جميعها كانت مزينه بالفسيفسه وساير مخادعها ودهاليزها فوق واسفل وذكروا ان كان يا سبعون هيكل فوق واسفل فلما اخريوها اللا المذكورين_اقامت نحو سعون سنه خرابًا الي ان ظهن المرحوم بطرس المدعو موهيلو اءني اخ موسي بيك الغضان وصار مطرانً على بلاد الروس سعا بترميمها بجسب المكنه على ما هى عليه الان رحمه الله
Let us return to the history of Kiev and the church of Saint Sophia. The church of Saint Sophia is built on the model of the famous church of that name which is in Constantinople, as much for its arches, as for its circular wall and its terrace; the mind cannot retain the innumerable variety of its marbles, the details of its construction and its proportions, the multitude of its columns, its elevation, the height of its domes, its vast dimensions, the multitude of its galleries, of its rooms. That one has the name and the reality, this one has the name and the quality. It is of square construction, and its dome is all in stone and bricks plastered with lime inside and outside, but starting from its western narthex, the other half is in ruins. It is said that a long time ago the Tatars burned and ruined it; it remained in ruins for about a hundred years, serving as shelter for animals and beasts of burden. It was rebuilt, then it was destroyed by the 'Uniates' or Russians subject to the Pope who removed all its paving and its mosaic which they transported to their churches. We were told that the church was entirely adorned with mosaics as well as all the rooms and the vestibules, from top to bottom. It is said that it had seventy sanctuaries, from top to bottom. When the Poles of whom we have spoken had ruined it, it remained in ruins for nearly seventy years, until the time of the late Peter Movila, brother of Moses, prince of Moldavia, who became metropolitan of the Russians and who worked to restore it as much as possible and as it still exists today. May God give rest to his soul!


[p. 713] {#po.26.p713}
وعلي يمين الداخل لبابها الغربي الان هيكلان خراب مبجوران الواحد على اسم العماد الألبى اى الغطاس وبه حجرن. معموديه للكاملين من الناس من حجر احمر صلب سماقي والاخر داخله عمار باقي وباب هذا الاب الكبير الغربي بابان اخران صثيران من اليمين والشمال وفي كل قرنه من القرائي الغرييه برج معظم مدور شاهقات اله بمرامي كثيرة حربيه وكل واحد وله باب غربي تطلع منه بدرج واسع عريض طويل كثير العدد غربي فوقاني تنفد منه الى رواق الكنيسه الداير الفوقانى التانى واما من جهة القبله فكان لبا خسه ابواب جمله واما من نهة الشمال فلها الان بابان واحدهم الان طريق مشاع عمال وفوقه صورة آجيا صوفيا على التكايسه والمسيح وشعاع روح قدسه حالل على الكنيسة واما الكاشيدياري والاعجام بشاشاتهم وبسمامهم وقيسيم يوتروها لنحو الكنيسة والفرئج بمداقمم وتفنكيم يحاربوها ولما في الجهة الشرقيه فلا سبعة حنابا كباد عليات الا ان الارعه سوا والثلاثه واميات اما في الرواق الفوقاني ففيه هيكلان ايض الواحد مقابل الثاني وجميييم في حناباهم شبابيك كثيرة كبار وكليم ببلور صافي حتي والطاقات الصغار وكل هيكل وفوقه قبه عاليه بصليب مطلي .يبرق آلا ان البيكل الكبير عالي جد وفي الجو شاهق وفي جانبيه بدنتين كبار عاليات بمزالق وطاهرة جميعه شبابيك مسدوده بكلس وجبصين وداخلبم صور وايقونات القديسين وعدد الركايز والبدنات التي من كل جوائب حيطانها عشرة واما طول الكنيسه فمايتي قنم وعشرة واما عرشها فكثر لان هواياتا اغرر وفوق شرافات حيطانها مبني مثل قناطر بصناعه عجيبه دقيقه علي طقس ددابزين اجيا صوفيا الحجري علي الحقيقه واما هوآات الكنيسه داخلبا من المغرب الي المشرق غزيرلا فعددهم أتني, عثر على صف واحد بالركايز المتينه والقناطر الكثيرة واذا .دخات الها من ابواها القرييه فتنظر جميع ارش الخورسص ضيفسه عجيب بنون الصناعة الدقيقه وكذلك داخل البياكل وقدامهم كثيرةٍ القيمه كذلك وفي الخورص داير خشب علي التربيع بثلاث درجات مجللة بجوخ احمن بديع وفوق الخورص المدكور القبة العاليه دات القماري الكثيه وهم انني عشر دايرها وفي علاها صدرة السيد المسيح والملابكة وتلاميده الاثني عش دايرها ثم في اربع قراينها الاررعة الانجيله وجميع ذلك بفسيفه
On the right, when one enters through the west door, one finds two ruined, abandoned sanctuaries, one is dedicated to the Baptism of the Lord, that is to say to the Epiphany; one sees there a baptistery for adults; it is built of red stone and hard as porphyry, the interior of the second is of a well-preserved construction. Near this large west door, there are two other small doors situated on the right and on the left. In each of the western corners of this church, there is a large, round, slender tower, provided with numerous strategic loopholes. Each tower has a door to the west, by which one climbs by a spacious, wide, very long staircase, with numerous steps, by which one arrives in the second upper gallery of the church. On the south side, the church had five doors in all, and on the north side it currently has only two, one of which serves for the common passage of the service staff. Above this door, on the lime plaster, Saint Sophia, Our Lord Jesus Christ, and the rays of the Holy Spirit descending on the church have been represented. The 'Kachidbary' and the Persians, with their turbans, with their bows and arrows, are drawing them to shoot at the church and the Europeans with their cannons and their rifles are fighting them. On the east side, there are seven large, high apses, four of which are of the same dimensions and three others lower. In the upper gallery, there are two sanctuaries situated one opposite the other. In all the apses there are numerous and large windows, each furnished with beautiful stained glass of pure crystal as well as small windows. Each sanctuary is surmounted by a high dome with a cross that shines like lightning. The great sanctuary is very high and soars into space. On both sides, there are two large high pilasters with slides (?). Outside the sanctuary, there are only windows blocked with lime and plaster. Inside, there are paintings and icons of the saints; the buttresses on all sides of the wall are ten in number. The length of the church is two hundred paces, its width is more, its divisions are numerous: above the battlements of its walls one sees a construction in the form of arcades of wonderful and fine work, on the model of the balustrades of Saint Sophia, in real stone. The divisions of the church inside from west to east are twelve in number in a single row with solid pillars and numerous arches. When one enters through the west doors, one sees the entire floor of the choir paved in wonderful mosaic, of fine and careful work. It is the same inside, and in front of the sanctuaries there are precious works. All around, inside the choir, there is a square wooden platform, with three steps covered with very pretty red cloth. Above this same choir is the high dome with numerous round skylights, twelve in number all around. On its ceiling there is a painting representing Our Lord Jesus Christ, the angels and his twelve apostles. In the four corners one sees the four Evangelists in beautiful royal gilded mosaic, with wonderful designs and


[p. 714] {#po.26.p714}


[p. 715] {#po.26.p715}
مدهبة ملوكية نقية بنقوشات عجيبة وكتابات يونانية وكذلك القناطر الارسة والرواقات التي فى هذا الجبه
Greek inscriptions. It is the same in the four galleries that are on this same side.


[p. 716] {#po.26.p716}
واما البيكل المعظم المشابه هيكل احا صوفيا المكرم في طوله واتساعه ومرضه دفي تلوين الفسيفسه في حيطانه وارضه فبو من اعلا الحنيه الي اسغلبا صودة السيدة بطوليا واقفة وفى زنارها منديليا فائحه كنها رافته .يديا أبغاية اللطافه ؤهى من يفيه ملوله ومدهيه عفافه لاننا نظرنا في هيكل آجيا صونيا الاصلي السيد السيح واقف بطوله بيبارك بغاية الكمال والزرافه وهاهنا تحت العدري البتول صورة مايدة العثا السري والملايكه حولبا والسيد بيناول علي يمينه لبطرس ورفقته الخز الالبي قايلًا والكلام الجوهري خارج من فمه الآلبي بالاحرف البونانيه الكبار خدوا كلوا هنا هو جدي تمامها ثم صورته تان بيسقيهم من الكاس علي شمال الا اشريوا من هذا كلكم بتمامبا ونحت المايدة الالبيه المدكوره ثلاث شبابيك معظمات وبين الشباكان صورة الكسيوس وبطرس مطارنة كيوف القدما” القديسان ببدلاتهم ولواطيهم بيض مزركثين ومكتوب
The great sanctuary resembles that of Saint Sophia in its length, its width and its extent, in the colors of the mosaics of its walls and its paving. Going from the ceiling of the vault down to the bottom, a painting represents Our Lady full-length, standing and having her veil held at the waist, the palms of her hands open and raised with grace; the painting is in mosaic, of different colors and gilded. We saw in the sanctuary of the original Saint Sophia, Our Lord Jesus Christ standing and blessing, of perfect and elegant execution; here, below the Blessed Virgin there is a painting representing the table of the Last Supper with angels all around. With his right hand the Lord distributes the divine bread to Peter and his companions while pronouncing the words of consecration which come out of his divine mouth, in large Greek characters: 'take, eat, this is my body, etc.'; in a second painting situated on the left, the Lord has been represented making them drink from the cup while saying: 'drink from it all' and the rest of the words. Below this painting of the Last Supper, there are three large windows between which one sees the portraits of Saint Alexis and Saint Peter, both ancient metropolitans of Kiev dressed in their vestments and their white 'latieh' in gold brocade, with their names in Greek.
عليهم باليوناني اسمايهم من على .ينينهم ستافانوس الارشيدياكون بالسخره وعلي صفة روسآة الكبنه ببدلاتهم منوره وهم غرشوربوس العجايبي وغرثوربوس نيصص ويوحنا فم الدهب وباسيليوس ثم علي الجبة الشمالنه بقرب الشباك صورة الشماس لفرنديوس ونيقولاس ميرا وغريخوربوس الاولوغس وكليميس بايا روميه وابيفانيوس قبرص وجميع صورهم ضيه مدهيه بكتابة يونانيه
To their right is the archdeacon Stephen with the censer and on the same row the archbishops dressed in their vestments have been represented; Gregory the Wonderworker, Gregory of Nyssa, John Chrysostom and Basil. On the left side near the window one sees the portrait of the deacon Lawrence and of Nicholas of Myra [in Lycia], Gregory the Theologian, Clement Pope of Rome and Epiphanius of Cyprus. All these portraits are in gilded mosaic with Greek inscriptions.
واما دايرة الكاتدرا فهى ثلاث درحات عاليات والكرسى فى الصدر وعدد درحه سته مرتفعات وداير ما يدورة مقدار نصف قامه تمام مرخم باصناف الرخام والفسيفسه العجية الالوان واما تقناطى البياكل وقبة الليه المجيبين فجمييم ضيه مدعيه سور القديسين
The platform of the throne has three high steps; the throne itself is on the front, with six high steps. All around, at the height of a man, there are different species of marble and superb mosaics. The arcades of the sanctuaries and the dome are very high and magnificent, they are all covered with gilded mosaic and images of saints.


[p. 717] {#po.26.p717}
